# Cobblemon Dynamics 1.0 spec sheet

Status: agreed (2026-11-09). Approver: Trevor Forbes (GitHub: HonorCodes). Rows prefix: CD.

Corrections after agreement:

- 2026-11-09: CD-CFG-01 and the term "server config" name the `config/` folder, because NeoForge 21.1 writes
  server configs there (found in M1).
- 2026-11-10, approver's play-test ruling: CD-HEAL-15 spawns only `cobblemon:heal_circles`. The file of
  `cobblemon:heal_sparkles` calls `math.random()` with no arguments, and the Cobblemon 1.8.4 client crashes on it.
- 2026-11-10, approver's play-test ruling: the Role table gives the Stoker and the Burner Keeper Ember instead of
  Flamethrower, which was too flashy for a loop. CD-FX-02 checks the role files against it.
- 2026-11-10, approver's play-test ruling: CD-FX-01 and CD-FX-03 let each work animation play to its end, then idle for
  `idle_ticks` before the next one. A loop every 40 ticks cut off animations of 3 s and started them again.
- 2026-11-10, approver's play-test ruling: all work is silent. CD-FX-03, CD-FX-09, CD-HEAL-15 and CD-CFG-06 drop the
  `cry` fallback of R46, every work sound and the `workSounds` client option of R21. The cry animation played the cry
  of each model with no attack animation on every loop.
- 2026-11-10, approver's play-test ruling: CD-ASG-09 prefers a work spot beside the target at its base height and never
  picks one that stands on the target. Before, the spot nearest to the worker won, which could be on top of the target. A
  spot that a worker cannot walk into on the level, such as a pit beside a water wheel, comes last.
- 2026-11-10, approver's play-test result: the second play-test of the Phase 1 build, on the jar of PR #12, is GREEN.
  The play-test note in `docs/testing/phase-1-gate.md` records it, so CD-REL-13 is complete. No row changes.
- 2026-11-10, approver's ruling after the play-test (R59): Fire-types do not heat Blaze Burners. The Burner Keeper role
  and its role file are gone, so the Role table, G3, the M3 scope and R15 lose it. The BRN rows keep their ids and now
  describe the Superheater: CD-BRN-01 holds `KINDLED` below level 50, and CD-BRN-05 counts 1 boiler heat from a
  Superheater below level 50. CD-SUP-02 adds that a Fire-type never claims a Blaze Burner, and the burner case of
  CD-ASG-16 now gives the Superheater as the burner's only role.
- 2026-11-10, approver's ruling after the play-test (R60): a Charge Cell block. New rows CD-GEN-08 to CD-GEN-12 cover
  its capacity and faces, its push into the blocks around it, its comparator signal, the FE that its item keeps, and its
  recipe, which the Recipes table lists. CD-REL-08 adds the Charge Cell to the player guide, and CD-ART-01 adds its art
  to the art of M8.
- 2026-11-10, approver's ruling after the play-test (R61): a creative tab. The new row CD-UI-16 lists every item of the
  mod in the tab `cobblemon_dynamics:main`, and in no vanilla tab.
- 2026-11-10, approver's ruling after the multiplayer audit (R62): an operator command for a binding whose binder does not
  come back. The new row CD-UI-17 gives `/cobblemondynamics unbind <pos>`, which clears every binding whose target or
  Telekinesis source is that block and tells each binder.
- 2026-11-10, approver's ruling after the multiplayer audit: CD-LSH-05 names a Cobblemon of another player by its species,
  as "Already assigned to another player's {species}", so a refusal never shows another player's nickname.
- 2026-11-10, approver's ruling after the multiplayer audit: CD-LSH-13 gives an owner who is offline the message at the next
  login. The server keeps up to the 10 newest such messages per owner in memory, so a server restart drops them.
- 2026-11-10, approver's art ruling: "the block art can just be a representative of the actual 3d object, like it
  currently is for the beds -- things like the work leash that you never place, are the only ones that need better art."
  CD-ART-01, CD-ART-02 and CD-ART-12 make block art that represents the placed 3D object, as the beds have it, release art.
  Original pixel art in Cobblemon's item style is for items that are never placed, which is the Work Leash. CD-ART-03,
  CD-ART-04 and the review of every asset (CD-ART-10) stay.
- 2026-11-10, ruling on the Phase 2 plan (delegated by the approver): CD-LSH-16 picks, among roles of one handler with the
  same priority, the role of the worker's primary type, then its secondary type, before the id order. A Ghost/Fire
  Chandelure is therefore a Ghost catalyst. The Slowbro case (Pump and Telekinesis are different handlers) is unchanged.
- 2026-11-11, approver's art review ruling: "for art review, can you just create a .png spritesheet and tell me the
  location? I'll review it that way". The review artifact of CD-ART-02, CD-ART-03, CD-ART-04 and CD-ART-10 is one PNG
  spritesheet, `build/art-review/spritesheet.png`, which `py scripts/art.py review` writes. It replaces the screenshot
  review page. The review record keeps the approver's verdicts, as before.
- 2026-11-11, the approver's request (R63), with the coordinator's rulings, which the approver delegated: Quick Work. A party
  Cobblemon that its owner sends out while the crosshair is on a block that one of its auto-assign roles works takes a one-shot
  work order on that block. The new Phase 2 area QWK (CD-QWK-01 to CD-QWK-25) and the new rows CD-CFG-16 to CD-CFG-18 hold
  it. CD-ASG-01 also gives claims to a Cobblemon on a Quick Work order, and CD-REL-08 adds Quick Work to the player guide. The
  non-goal of work by party Cobblemon now excepts Quick Work, and the Terms add Quick Work order, Quick Worker and aimed block.
- 2026-11-11, editorial: CD-LSH-16 states its rule with one keyword. Its meaning is unchanged.
- 2026-11-11, coordinator's ruling under the approver's delegation: CD-QWK-21 has no GameTest. Cobblemon 1.8.4 clears a
  battle id that names no live battle on the entity's next tick, and a test battle against a fake owner ends in one tick.
  The unit tests of the trace range rule and the end rule in `QuickWorkRulesTest`, and a Demo of the manual checklist,
  check the row. Its requirement is unchanged.
- 2026-11-11, the approver's ruling on land claims (R64), with the coordinator's rulings under the approver's
  delegation: Quick Work respects the land claims of FTB Chunks and Open Parties and Claims. The new rows CD-QWK-26 to
  CD-QWK-35 hold it. The engine asks each loaded claim mod before an order starts and every 20 ticks while it runs, a
  check that throws counts as a refusal, and the compat classes load only with their mod. CD-QWK-23 adds the checks of
  CD-QWK-29 to the cost of an order's tick. The Terms add claim mod and land claim. By the coordinator's rulings on the
  design, the same day, CD-QWK-34 declares both claim mods as optional dependencies with the version range `*`. The work
  areas of pastures keep their Phase 1 behavior: the Phase 2 gate report lists them as a known issue, and the open
  question Q9 holds the decision for Phase 3, because the approver has not ruled on that part of the scope of 1.0.
- 2026-11-11, the request of COHO-2 (R65), with the coordinator's ruling that it ships in the same build: the new rows
  CD-CMP-08 to CD-CMP-10 log the mixins, the payloads and the roles at load, in one INFO line each. They are Phase 2
  work in a Phase 1 area, as CD-CFG-16 to CD-CFG-18 are, so the coverage script and the gate report count them as
  Phase 2 rows.
- 2026-11-11, the approver's ruling on shared containers (R66), with the coordinator's rulings under the approver's
  delegation: the logistics roles, the Magnet and Telekinesis, share their containers. The approver:

  > one bug: the ideal flow for chest transfer cobblemon is collecting into a chest, then transfering to another
  > container, which requires AT LEAST two claims ont he same chest:
  > - steel: collect ground loot to chest
  > - psychic: transfer from the same chest that loot is collected to, to a different chest
  >
  > I think that means for logistic-type interactions, there shouldn't be a claim limit (you can assign more than 1
  > collector/transfer cobblemon to speed up both)

  And, the same day: "it may also be deposit and collection location. basically anything logicstic shouldn't have a claim
  cap (or the cap should be 3~5, enough that it wouldn't realistically be hit per object)". The coordinator ruled that
  every container of a logistics role is shared, with a cap of 5 workers per container, counted across the logistics
  roles together at the canonical position, in the new server config key `logistics.maxWorkersPerContainer`. The new
  rows CD-CLM-10, CD-CLM-11, CD-LSH-28, CD-LSH-29, CD-TLK-13, CD-MAG-09 and CD-CFG-19 hold the ruling. CD-CLM-01,
  CD-CLM-05, CD-CLM-06, CD-LSH-05 and CD-QWK-07 now apply only to exclusive claims and bindings, CD-TLK-05 makes the
  Telekinesis claim a shared claim, and the Terms add logistics role and place. Machines keep one worker per block.
  CD-CLM-10, CD-CLM-11, CD-LSH-28, CD-LSH-29 and CD-CFG-19 are Phase 2 work in Phase 1 areas, as CD-CFG-16 to CD-CFG-18
  are, so the coverage script counts them as Phase 2 rows.
- 2026-11-11, the coordinator's rulings on the open points of R66, under the approver's delegation: a Telekinesis
  destination takes a place at bind time only, and a lower `logistics.maxWorkersPerContainer` clears no binding.
  CD-TLK-05's many workers that feed one destination stop at the cap, under the approver's cap of 3 to 5. By CD-CLM-11,
  a Stoker and a Telekinesis worker that empties the same furnace work side by side, and a Quick Work order starts on
  a block that only logistics workers hold (CD-QWK-07). Amendment 1 of ADR-0005 records the shared logistics claims.
  No row changes.
- 2026-11-11, the approver's ruling (R67): "yes, ship beta.3; then work on beta.4 while we play. make beta.4 the actual
  release, not a beta anymore. and make sure we have all planned features in." The next version is 1.0.0, the public
  release of Phase 3. With the ruling, the approver approved two features. The workers of a Work Mode pasture respect
  land claims by the rights of the pasture's placer, also while the placer is offline. MineColonies joins FTB Chunks and
  Open Parties and Claims as a claim mod, for Quick Work and for pastures. The new Phase 3 area LND (CD-LND-01 to
  CD-LND-16) holds both features, and its rows stay `draft` until the coordinator rules on the design calls of
  ADR-0014. The terms claim mod and land claim now include MineColonies and its colonies, so CD-QWK-26, CD-QWK-29 and
  CD-QWK-30 also cover MineColonies. The ruling answers open question Q9. R68 holds the MineColonies jars and settings
  of COHO 2.4.0, RES7 holds the research, and the Milestones table gives M9 its new scope.
- 2026-11-11, the approver's question on publication and the coordinator's ruling (R69): CD-REL-04 no longer needs the
  tokens and project ids of Modrinth and CurseForge. The approver: "why does it need project ids and api tokens? I
  already released it with the beta.2 build and it just required the JAR". A public tag builds the jar and attaches it
  to the GitHub release. The publish job skips each site whose token or project id is absent, with a notice, instead of
  failing. A site whose settings are present still gets the jar from the job.
- 2026-11-11, the coordinator's rulings on the design calls of the Phase 3 plan, under the approver's delegation: calls 1
  to 11, 13, 15 and 16 stand as the plan wrote them, so CD-LND-01 to CD-LND-16 move to `agreed`, and ADR-0014 is
  accepted. Call 14 changed. By the approver's ruling of 2026-11-10 ("no multiplayer game tests, we don't have that
  capacity"), CD-UI-03 and CD-UI-09 drop their Demos with two players. Their acceptance tests are now their unit tests,
  the GameTest of CD-UI-09 and a code reading against the multiplayer rules, as the multiplayer audit reads the code.
  Calls 12 (Modrinth) and 17 (a play-test of the 1.0.0 candidate) wait for the approver, and no row depends on them.
- 2026-11-11, the approver's ruling (R70), with the coordinator's rulings under the approver's delegation: a Fairy-type
  works an enchanting table as the Muse on a Quick Work order. The approver: "fairy types should be able to be thrown out from inventory at enchantment tables to grant it the bonus that they'd normally grant in the pasture/working (similarly to how fire tpyes can be thrown out at furnances to power them from player inventory)". A role file field,
  `quick_work`, lets a role whose auto-assign flag is false take Quick Work orders, and only the Muse's file sets it. The
  new row CD-QWK-36 holds the flag. CD-QWK-05 gives a Fairy-type an order only for such a role, and a Quick Worker still
  takes no heal claim. CD-QWK-09 and CD-QWK-19 except such an order: it starts while the table's menu is closed, and its
  worker waits in Standby with no Standby end, because the owner cannot press R with a screen open and often closes the
  table to fetch lapis or books. CD-CFG-07 adds the flag to the role file, and the rationale of CD-MUS-02 names the order.
- 2026-11-11, the coordinator's ruling on call 13 of the Phase 3 plan, under the approver's delegation: CD-STK-07 reads
  "keep the flame gauge full while the furnace cooks". It read "set the burn duration to 1,600 ticks". Since M1 the handler
  raises `litTime` to 2 ticks and sets `litDuration` to the same value while `litTime` is below 2, so the gauge reads
  full, and the approver play-tested that (check 29). The code does not change.
- 2026-11-11, the approver's ruling (R71), with the coordinator's rulings under the approver's delegation: where Apothic
  Enchanting is loaded, the Muse adds Quanta, not Eterna. The approver: "also the fairy types should give Quanta, not Eterna, if apotheosis is active (for enchanting). it seems like they currently give eterna bonus." Apothic Enchanting
  gives the vanilla enchanting table its own menu. That menu computes the offers from the table's stats and never reads
  the bookshelf count that CD-MUS-03 changes. So at such a table, a Muse added no bonus at all. The new row CD-MUS-07
  adds ceil(45 × L ÷ 100) Quanta to the stats that Apothic Enchanting gathers for the table, and no bookshelves.
  CD-MUS-03 excepts such a table. CD-MUS-06 also turns the Muse off when that stats hook does not apply. CD-CMP-08 names
  the hook among the skipped mixins of a server without Apothic Enchanting. CD-MUS-07 is Phase 3 work in a Phase 1
  area, so the coverage script counts it as a Phase 3 row.
- 2026-11-12, the approver's rulings on 1.1 (R72, R73), with the coordinator's rulings under the approver's delegation: the
  farming roles. The approver: "I was hoping to make the other one have the function of Bonemeal/quick growth for
  applicable crops in range", "I also wanted to have Grass-types harvest grown crops (not apricorns, though), same leash

- 2026-11-12, editorial: the Inputs table gains ADR 0014, which the trace table already mapped.

  approver's answers to Q10 and Q11.
- 2026-11-12, the approver's answers to Q10 and Q11 (R75): "1. no" and "2. accept". The Cultivator grows no apricorns,

- 2026-11-12, the 1.1 build: CD-LSH-08, CD-ASG-12, CD-ASG-19 and CD-LND-12 gain pointers to the 1.1 rows that except them,

- 2026-11-15, the approver's rulings on 1.2 (R76, R77, R78), with the coordinator's rulings under the approver's

  to CD-AIR-08), KIN (CD-KIN-01 to CD-KIN-06), FRM (CD-FRM-01 to CD-FRM-08) and MCH (CD-MCH-01 to CD-MCH-12), and

The key words "MUST", "MUST NOT", "SHOULD", "SHOULD NOT" and "MAY" in this document are to be
interpreted as described in BCP 14 (RFC 2119, RFC 8174) when, and only when, they appear in all
capitals, as shown here.

This sheet lists what Cobblemon Dynamics 1.0 does. 1.0 is the first public release. It ships in
three phases (see the Milestones table and the REL rows). Phase 1 builds and tests the v1 scope
privately. Phase 2 adds the v2 roles, Quick Work and the final art, and is also tested privately.
Phase 3 adds the land claim checks of pastures and MineColonies, and then publishes. Rows in the TLK,
MAG, FAN, TLW, QWK and ART areas belong to Phase 2, and so do CD-CFG-16 to CD-CFG-19, CD-CMP-08 to
CD-CMP-10, CD-CLM-10, CD-CLM-11, CD-LSH-28 and CD-LSH-29. Rows in the LND area belong to Phase 3, and so do
CD-QWK-36 and CD-MUS-07. All other rows belong to Phase 1, and the BAL rows apply to the roles of each phase.

Version 1.1 follows the public release 1.0.0. It adds two farming roles, the Cultivator and the Harvester, and the crop
adapters that let them work the crops of other mods (R72, R73, milestone M10). Rows in the CUL, HRV and CRP areas belong
to 1.1, and so do CD-BAL-08, CD-BAL-09 and CD-REL-16. The coordinator agreed the rows of 1.1 on 2026-11-12 (R74), and the approver agreed the last six,
CD-CUL-04, CD-CUL-06, CD-CUL-08, CD-CUL-10, CD-CRP-01 and CD-BAL-08, the same day with the answers to Q10 and Q11
(R75).

Version 1.2 follows 1.1.0. It adds four roles, the Lumberjack, the Soul Collector, the Freezer and the Heater, the
Experience Collector block, the machines and crops of other mods for the existing roles with no setup, and fixes of
today's behaviour (R76 to R80, milestone M11). Rows in the OPT, FIX, LUM, XPC, SOU, FRZ, HTR, FUEL, AIR, KIN, FRM and MCH
areas belong to 1.2, and so do CD-BAL-10 to CD-BAL-17, CD-CFG-20 and CD-REL-17. Every row of 1.2 is `draft` until the
coordinator rules on the design decisions of the [1.2 plan](2026-11-15-v1.2-new-roles-and-compat.md#design-decisions).

Each row states one behaviour and holds the test that proves it. A phase is done when every MUST
and MUST NOT row in its scope has a passing test. Nobody builds against a `draft` row. The approver
moves rows to `agreed`.

Design context lives in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/roles.md`, the ADRs in `docs/adr/` and the
research notes in `docs/research/2026-11-09-integration-points.md`.

## Terms

| Term | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Work Mode | A per-pasture setting. While it is on, Dynamics controls the owned Cobblemon that are tethered to that pasture. |
| work area | The box around a pasture block in which its workers find targets and beds. By default it reaches 8 blocks horizontally and 6 blocks vertically from the pasture block in each direction. |
| worker | A Cobblemon tethered to a pasture in Work Mode. A healer is a worker too. |
| target | The block or entity that a worker acts on: a machine, a crop, a bed or a healed ally. |
| canonical target | The one position that stands for a multiblock target, for example the controller of a large water wheel. |
| claim | A claim registry entry that gives one worker one target for now. The kinds are the work claim (on a machine or a container), the bed claim (on a bed) and the heal claim (on an ally). The work engine makes every claim. Claims are never saved. Every claim is an exclusive claim, except the work claim of a logistics role, which is a shared claim (CD-CLM-10). |
| leash binding | A reservation that a player makes with the Work Leash. It is not a claim. It is saved with the Cobblemon, and it reserves its target for that worker, also while the worker sleeps or its pasture's Work Mode is off. A binding of a logistics role reserves no target. It takes a place at each container that it names (CD-LSH-29). |
| logistics role | A role whose handler moves items into or out of containers and shares its targets: the Magnet and Telekinesis. A role file that names such a handler is a logistics role too. Since 1.2, the Soul Collector, which moves XP into an Experience Collector, is a logistics role too. Every role that is neither a logistics role nor a farming role is an exclusive role, and its claims and bindings are exclusive. |
| farming role | The Cultivator or the Harvester (1.1). On a crop, the claims and bindings of one farming role never block those of the other farming role (CD-CRP-06, CD-CRP-07). A role file that names the handler of either is a farming role too. Since 1.2, a role whose handler claims in a farming lane, such as a pot role of Botany Pots, is a farming role too. |
| crop | A block of the tag `cobblemon_dynamics:cultivator_targets` or `cobblemon_dynamics:harvester_targets` that a crop adapter reads for that role. A crop of two blocks, such as hemp, is one crop at its lower block. Since 1.2, a Botany Pot that a pot role works is a crop too. |
| crop adapter | The code that reads one family of crops for the farming roles: whether a crop can still grow, whether it is fully grown, and what a harvest drops and leaves. The adapter table of ADR-0015 lists the adapters and the crop families that each one reads. |
| fully grown | The state in which a crop's adapter lets the Harvester harvest it, for example the maximum age of a `CropBlock`. A Cultivator stops there too. |
| growth use | One application of the bonemeal effect to a crop by a Cultivator. It uses no bone meal item and costs Work Stamina (CD-CUL-06). |
| replant item | A dropped item that places a crop's own block, a `BlockItem` whose `getBlock()` is that block, such as wheat seeds for wheat, a carrot for carrots or a Pam's crop item for its crop. |
| place | One of the `logistics.maxWorkersPerContainer` places of a container at its canonical position. Each binding of a logistics role takes one place at each container that it names: the Magnet's container, or the Telekinesis source and destination. |
| auto-assigned worker | A worker with no leash binding. The work engine picks its targets. |
| Quick Work order | A one-shot work order of a party Cobblemon. It starts when the owner sends the Cobblemon out while the crosshair is on a block that one of its auto-assign roles works. It holds one work claim, never searches for other work, and ends for good. The QWK rows hold its rules. |
| Quick Worker | A party Cobblemon on a Quick Work order. No pasture holds it, so a row about workers applies to it only where a QWK row says so. |
| aimed block | The block that the owner's view hit when the owner sent out a Quick Worker. The leash range of the order counts from it. |
| claim mod | FTB Chunks, Open Parties and Claims or MineColonies. Each protects the chunks that players or colonies claim with it, and decides who can use the blocks there. |
| land claim | A chunk that a claim mod protects, such as a chunk of a MineColonies colony. It is not a claim. |
| colony | A MineColonies colony. It claims the chunks around its center, and the ranks of its players decide who can use the blocks there. |
| pasture check | The question that the work engine asks each claim mod for a Work Mode pasture: may the pasture's placer, online or not, use a block with an empty main hand. The LND rows hold its rules. |
| break question | The question that the Harvester asks each claim mod before a harvest that breaks a block: may the pasture's placer, by UUID, or the online owner of a Quick Worker break that block. The rows CD-HRV-16 to CD-HRV-21 hold its rules. |
| binder | The owner of a worker that has a leash binding. |
| pending source | The first container of a Telekinesis binding, which the Work Leash holds while it waits for the destination click. |
| role | A job that a datapack role file defines: eligible types, an auto-assign flag, a priority, a range, a handler, handler parameters and an effect. |
| role file | The datapack JSON file of one role. It holds the role-specific numbers: rates, formula constants, level gates, ranges, effect ids and the priority. It holds no species lists. |
| server config | The NeoForge server config `cobblemon_dynamics-server.toml`. NeoForge 21.1 writes it to the instance's `config/` folder. A copy in a world's `serverconfig/` folder overrides it for that world. It holds the engine-wide numbers: work area, scan cadence and budget, stamina per level, bed factors, healer recovery and timeouts. |
| handler | The code that finds, checks and works one family of targets. |
| demand | The target can use work now, for example a furnace whose recipe can run. |
| driven target | A wheel, a hand crank or a fan that a worker is Working on. |
| Idle, Traveling, Working, Standby, ToBed, Sleeping, RestingInPlace, Healing | The worker states. A worker is in exactly one of them. Working means at the work spot with demand. Standby means the worker waits with no work: at the work spot with no demand, or, for a leashed worker, during the wait after a missed arrival. ToBed means walking to a claimed bed. Sleeping means lying on a bed. RestingInPlace means sleeping where the worker stands. Healing means a healer walks to an ally or heals it. |
| Tired | A flag, not a state. The stamina component sets it when Work Stamina reaches 0% and clears it at 100%. A Tired worker is in ToBed, Sleeping or RestingInPlace. |
| worker rule | Full recovery from 0% to 100% takes 3 min × level. |
| healer rule | Full recovery takes 4 × (300 − 270 × (L − 1) ÷ 99) min. It applies to a Cobblemon with a Fairy type and no machine binding. |
| work focus entity | An invisible, unsaved entity at a target's center. Only a Working worker has one. Cobblemon particles aim at it. |
| placer | The player that Cobblemon records in the pasture block entity's `ownerId` when the pasture is placed. `ownerId` is the one source of pasture ownership. |
| operator | A player with permission level 2 or higher. |
| legendary or mythical | A Cobblemon whose form carries the Cobblemon label `legendary` or `mythical`. |
| Phase 1, Phase 2, Phase 3 | The release phases: the private test of v1, the private test of v2, and the public release. The Milestones table lists their milestones. |
| 1.1 | The first version after the public release 1.0.0. It adds the farming roles (milestone M10). |
| 1.2 | The second version after the public release 1.0.0. It adds the Lumberjack, the Soul Collector, the Freezer, the Heater, the Experience Collector, and the machines and crops of other mods (milestone M11). |
| optional mod | A mod of the table of ADR-0016. Dynamics works its machines or crops when it is loaded, with no setup, and loads no class of it when it is absent. |
| compat line | The INFO line of CD-OPT-11 that names each adapter of one loaded optional mod as found or missing. |
| tree | Logs of one block id with at least 3 natural leaves, which a Lumberjack fells whole (CD-LUM-04 to CD-LUM-08). |
| trunk base cell | The lowest log of a tree, or the corner with the least coordinates of a 2 × 2 trunk. A Lumberjack binding names it. |
| fell | The removal of a tree with its natural leaves and its shell, followed by its drops and its replant (CD-LUM-09 to CD-LUM-18). |
| shell | The blocks next to a tree that could not survive without it, such as cocoa, vines and torches (CD-LUM-09). |
| Experience Collector | The block of 1.2 that stores XP, gives it to a player through a button, and offers it to pipes (the XPC rows). |
| orb unit | One pickup of an XP orb, worth its `value`. An orb with a `count` of 4 holds 4 units. |
| freezable cell | A water source under air in the work area that none of CD-FRZ-06 to CD-FRZ-09, CD-FRZ-15 and CD-FRZ-16 excludes. |

## Goals

- G1: A player switches a Cobblemon pasture to Work Mode, and its owned Cobblemon power and operate machines in the work area, with no stand block.
- G2: Water-types turn Create water wheels with no water, Electric-types generate Forge Energy by level, and Fire-types fuel furnaces as if they burned coal.
- G3: Thirteen types get fifteen roles across vanilla Minecraft, Create and Applied Energistics 2: eleven in Phase 1 and four in Phase 2.
- G4: Only one worker acts on a target at a time, across all pastures and players, and wild Cobblemon never work.
- G5: A player binds a specific pastured Cobblemon to a specific machine with the Work Leash.
- G6: Work Stamina, four bed tiers and Fairy healers make worker uptime something that a player plans, as in Palworld.
- G7: Players see the state and Work Stamina of each worker in the world and in the pasture panel.
- G8: Dynamics runs in one modpack with Cobbleworkers and Cobblebase.
- G9: 1.0 ships as a public MPL-2.0 release on Modrinth and CurseForge after two private test phases, and joins the approver's modpack.
- G10: No single Cobblemon solves an engineering problem that the player is meant to manage. Cobblemon are an early-to-midgame option that a fleet can make competitive, and top-tier engineering always outperforms them.
- G11: Every new block, item and UI sprite uses original art in vanilla 16 × 16 style, and the approver approves each piece before the public release.
- G12: In 1.1, Ground-types grow crops and Grass-types harvest and replant them, for vanilla crops and the crop mods of the COHO modpack, with a leash for one crop or none for a whole field.
- G13: In 1.2, Bug-types fell and replant trees, Ghost-types collect experience into an Experience Collector, Ice-types freeze surface water, and Fire-types heat the heat machines of Mekanism and PneumaticCraft.
- G14: In 1.2, the existing roles also work the machines and crops of the mods of ADR-0016, with no setup by the server admin.

## Non-goals

- A Cobblemon Work Stand block. Work Mode on the pasture replaces it (ADR 0001).
- Fabric, Forge, and Minecraft versions other than 1.21.1 (ADR 0002).
- Jobs that make items from nothing. Cobbleworkers, Cobblebase and Cobblemon Pasture Loot cover loot.
- The later idea: the Pollinator (Bug). `docs/roles.md` holds it. In 1.1, the Harvester (R72) takes the place of the
  Grower (Grass) idea, and in 1.2 the Freezer (R76) leaves the list.
- A role for the Dark type. The Dark type has no role. Since 1.1, Ground-types are Cultivators (R72).
- Create valve handles. Create turns a valve handle only by a fixed angle, so a valve handle cannot give continuous power.
- Work by party Cobblemon or by Cobblemon that a player sent out, except a Quick Work order (R63, the QWK rows). Outside a
  Quick Work order, only pastured Cobblemon work.
- Effects of Work Stamina on battles.
- Level scaling for roles other than the Generator, Pump, Muse, Telekinesis, Magnet, Tailwind and, in 1.1, the Cultivator
  and, in 1.2, the Soul Collector, the Freezer, the Heater and the targets of ADR-0020 that name a level, and the level
  gates of the Superheater and of the blast furnaces of CD-FUEL-02 and CD-FUEL-04.
- A harvest into a container. The Harvester drops its harvest on the ground beside the crop, where a Magnet collects it (R73).
- Crop families outside the adapter tables of ADR-0015 and ADR-0020 as default targets, for example trees, sugar cane,
  cactus, bamboo, kelp, mushrooms and flowers. Since 1.2, the Cultivator grows saplings (CD-LUM-23). A pack adds a block to a tag of the farming roles, and a crop adapter then reads it
  (CD-CRP-03).
- The Rancher (Normal), the Ice Quencher and the fan catalysts of Create: Dragons Plus, Create: Enchantment Industry and
  Create: Aquatic Ambitions (R76, R80).
- A bottle mode of the Soul Collector, a Soul Collector target in the tank of another mod, and an XP fluid of our own
  (R79). The XP machines of other mods take the Experience Collector's output through pipes.
- Sanding by Ground-types, coke ovens, the AE2 Vibration Chamber, PneumaticCraft cooling for Ice, the XP Tome and free fuel
  for the solid-fuel FE generators of other mods (R80).
- Mods with no NeoForge 1.21.1 build: Thermal Expansion, Thermal Dynamics, Tinkers' Construct and Botania (R80).
- A land claim question about an entity. As the Magnet takes items, the Soul Collector takes the orbs of its work area
  without one.
- An output cap beyond the values in role files and the server config. A pack that raises a rate gets that rate.
- A server-only install. The mod adds items, blocks and screens, so clients need it too.
- Work suitability tables per species. Eligibility comes from the types in role files, and role files hold no species lists.

## Inputs

Rulings R1 to R58 come from the design session with the approver on 2026-11-09. R59 to R61 come from the approver's rulings after the Phase 1 play-test, on 2026-11-10. R62 comes from the approver's rulings on the multiplayer audit, on 2026-11-10. R63 comes from the approver's request of 2026-11-11 and the coordinator's rulings on it, which the approver delegated. R64 comes from the approver's ruling of 2026-11-11 on land claims and the coordinator's rulings on it, which the approver delegated. R65 comes from the request of COHO-2, the session that maintains the COHO modpack, of 2026-11-11 and the coordinator's ruling on it. R66 comes from the approver's ruling of 2026-11-11 on shared containers and the coordinator's rulings on it, which the approver delegated. R72 and R73 come from the approver's rulings of 2026-11-12 on the farming roles of 1.1 and the coordinator's rulings on them, which the approver delegated. R75 comes from the approver's answers of 2026-11-12 to Q10 and Q11. R76 to R78 come from the approver's rulings of 2026-11-15 on 1.2, and R79 and R80 from the coordinator's rulings on them, which the approver delegated.

| R63. Request (2026-11-11), with the coordinator's rulings, which the approver delegated ("go with your recommendations all the way through"): Quick Work. The approver: "the ability for a user to throw out a cobblemon with R as they normally do, but if their crosshair is targeting something that the selected cobblemon/that they are putting out can work, then it will start to work it. For example, if I have charmander selected, look at a smelter, then press R to bring it out, charmander should attempt to power it; with it leashing away from the smelter if I walk too far away. it shouldn't keep assessing for work after being leashed away and/or recalled; so it shouldn't take TPS for that. this is basically an on-demand use of this mod with in-party cobblemon that are summoned while targeting an applicable machine/thing." And: "it also shouldn't work with machines that require the cobblemon dynamics leash item (like more complex behaviors, like item transfers, etc.)". The rulings: 1. The server traces the view at a send-out outside a battle, from the eye position with the server's rotation, up to `quickWork.targetRange` blocks (default 16), and takes the first block hit. No client change is needed. 2. Only roles with `auto_assign: true` and one target count, never healers. The role is chosen as an auto claim chooses it, and the block must match the role and have demand when the order starts. 3. The owner must be allowed to interact with the block, by `mayInteract`, and the order takes a normal work claim. A claimed target or a Tired Cobblemon gives no order and an action-bar message. 4. The order is one-shot. It ends for good when the owner is farther than `quickWork.leashRange` blocks (default 16) from the target, on a recall, when the Cobblemon is Tired, when the target breaks or no longer matches, after the usual Standby time with no demand, when the owner logs out or changes dimension, and in a battle. The Cobblemon then follows its owner as usual, and action-bar messages say when work starts and when and why it stops. 5. No cost per tick after an order ends, and about one pasture worker's cost while it runs. 6. All state lives on the server, keyed by the Cobblemon's UUID with the owner's UUID, and a server stop clears it. No multiplayer GameTests. | ADR 0013 |
| R64. Ruling (2026-11-11), with the coordinator's rulings, which the approver delegated: Quick Work respects land claims. The approver: "yes, respect claims; must work with parties/clans (share chunks)". The rulings: 1. The claim mods are FTB Chunks, with the parties and allies of FTB Teams, and Open Parties and Claims, with its parties and allies. Both stay optional. The compat compiles against their APIs as compile-only dependencies, and only a guarded `register` loads a class that names one of them, as for Create. 2. Before an order starts, the engine asks each loaded claim mod whether the owner, an online `ServerPlayer`, may right-click the canonical block. The claim mod's own rules decide who shares a chunk, and a refusal reuses "You may not use {target}". 3. While an order runs, the engine asks again every 20 ticks, and it ends the order with that message when the permission is gone. 4. A check that throws, a `LinkageError` included, refuses, with one warning per cause. 5. No new config key. 6. The work areas of pastures stay out of scope, as a known issue. The coordinator's rulings on the design, the same day, add that both claim mods get optional entries with the version range `*` in `neoforge.mods.toml`, as Cobbleworkers and Cobblebase have, and that the work areas of pastures are an open decision for Phase 3 (Q9), not a non-goal. | ADR 0013 |
| R65. Request (2026-11-11) of COHO-2, with the coordinator's ruling that it ships in the same build as R64: three INFO lines at load. One names the mixins that applied and the mixins that were skipped, one names the registered payloads, and one counts the loaded roles and names each disabled role. | none |
| R66. Ruling (2026-11-11), with the coordinator's rulings, which the approver delegated: the logistics roles share their containers. The approver: "one bug: the ideal flow for chest transfer cobblemon is collecting into a chest, then transfering to another container, which requires AT LEAST two claims ont he same chest: - steel: collect ground loot to chest - psychic: transfer from the same chest that loot is collected to, to a different chest I think that means for logistic-type interactions, there shouldn't be a claim limit (you can assign more than 1 collector/transfer cobblemon to speed up both)". And: "it may also be deposit and collection location. basically anything logicstic shouldn't have a claim cap (or the cap should be 3~5, enough that it wouldn't realistically be hit per object)". The rulings: 1. Every container of a logistics role is shared: the Magnet's container, and the Telekinesis source and destination. 2. The cap is 5 logistics workers per container, counted across the logistics roles together, in the server config key `logistics.maxWorkersPerContainer` (1 to 16, default 5). A sixth worker gets no claim on the container, and its bind is refused with a clear message. 3. The cap counts per container at its canonical position, so a double chest or a Create item vault is one container (ruling 5 of the Phase 2 plan). | none |
| R67. Ruling (2026-11-11) of the approver: "yes, ship beta.3; then work on beta.4 while we play. make beta.4 the actual release, not a beta anymore. and make sure we have all planned features in." The next version is 1.0.0, a public release that holds every planned feature. With the ruling, the approver approved two features. 1. Claim checks for Work Mode pastures: the auto-assignment and the workers of a pasture never work a target in a chunk that the pasture's owner may not interact with. The owner may be offline, so the check works from the owner's UUID, with the team members, parties and allies of each claim mod, under that mod's own rules. Leash bindings already obey canceled clicks (CD-SEC-11). 2. MineColonies colony protection, for Quick Work and for pastures, beside FTB Chunks and Open Parties and Claims. The ruling answers open question Q9. | ADR 0014 |
| R68. Request (2026-11-11) of COHO-2, through the coordinator: build against the MineColonies jars of COHO 2.4.0 (pack tag 2.4.0-rc4): `minecolonies-1.1.1419-1.21.1-snapshot.jar`, whose API ships in the main jar, `structurize-1.0.839-1.21.1-snapshot.jar`, `blockui-1.0.212-1.21.1-snapshot.jar`, `domum-ornamentum-1.0.237-snapshot-main.jar` and `multipiston-1.2.58-1.21.1.jar`. COHO keeps the defaults of MineColonies: `enablecolonyprotection = true`, `turnoffexplosionsincolonies = "DAMAGE_ENTITIES"`, `permissioneventbypassminpermlevel = 2`, `pvp_mode = false` and `maxColonySize = 20`. Each colony keeps the permissions of its ranks in its world data, so a colony's own ranks decide who may use a block, as FTB Chunks and Open Parties and Claims decide through their own rules. | ADR 0014 |
| R69. Ruling (2026-11-11) of the coordinator on publication, after the approver's question: "why does it need project ids and api tokens? I already released it with the beta.2 build and it just required the JAR". The approver uploads the jar to CurseForge by hand. 1. A public `v*` tag still builds the jar and attaches it to the GitHub release. 2. The publish job skips Modrinth and CurseForge with a notice when their secrets or project ids are absent, instead of failing. 3. When the secrets are added later, the job publishes as before. 4. | R70. Grass-types are the Harvester. They harvest fully grown crops, drop everything on the ground, and replant from the drops: a seed, or the crop's own item. They never take apricorns. Leashing works as for the Cultivator. 4. Both roles are Quick Work eligible, as auto-assign roles. Work stays silent: no sounds. | `docs/adr/0015-farming-roles-and-crop-adapters.md` |

| R82. Rulings (2026-11-16) of the coordinator on the review of the Lumberjack branch, under the approver's delegation. A running fell holds a region ticket on each chunk of its plan. A block whose check or loot throws plans no fell or drops no loot, and never turns the role off. A cell of a chunk that is not loaded counts as a log, and a log next to one gives no tree. A leaf that is persistent when its step comes stays. The end of a fell asks the use question of the base cell again before the replant. The tag holds sugar cane, the coral blocks and corals, the conduit and Create's encased fan as an optional entry. 2. The occupant guard reads any entity except dropped items and XP orbs, in the cell and down its water column, at most 3 blocks or to the first cell that is not water (CD-FRZ-16). 3. A Working tick checks only the cheap facts of the claimed cell, and the farmland, adjacent-water and entity checks run on freeze ticks only. 4. The scan asks the demand of an idle worker's candidates nearest first and stops at the first with demand. 5. A worker drops its expired skips and stalls when a claim ends. 6. The perf diagnostic prints both maps. 7. A pace of 0 or less gives the Freezer no demand, and a fractional pace counts as its ceiling (CD-FRZ-04). | `docs/plans/2026-11-15-v1.2-new-roles-and-compat.md` |
| R85. Rulings (2026-11-16) of the coordinator, under the approver's delegation, on seven findings of the review of Tasks 10 to 12, and a ruling (2026-11-17) on the boiler's part of CD-FUEL-14. 1. An automatic Stoker would light a Railcraft Reborn boiler that the player never fuelled, and `SteamBoiler.checkFill` explodes a superheated boiler, at 300 or more, that takes water into an empty water tank. The firebox moves to its own role file, `stoker_railcraft_boiler`, Work Leash only, and the blast furnace stays automatic. A firebox block has demand only while the boiler's water fills at least half of its tank, so never while the boiler is superheated with less, and a misuse row records it. 2. `SteamBoiler.tick` throws away burn time below the need of a fuel step when it has no fuel item, so a cap of 1 burn tick per tick per worker could fuel only the boiler of 1 low-pressure tank. The workers of one boiler pool their supply: they fuel it only while every firebox block of the boiler has a bound worker, and then each covers its share, the need ÷ the firebox blocks of each fuel step, and the boiler burns no fuel item. With fewer workers, they wait in Standby with no demand, and the boiler burns its own fuel. 3. The Wheel Driver of IE has demand only while a block that exposes `IRotationAcceptor.CAPABILITY` sits in the wheel's row of at most 3 wheels along its axis, because IE gives power only through the wheel beside a Kinetic Dynamo. 4. Every end of a watermill worker's claim ends its drive, and a drive that its worker no longer renews lapses. 5. `heat_per_tick_at_level_100` is clamped to 0 to the heat that a full plant uses. 6. Every end of a windmill worker's claim ends its bonus. 7. The Fuelwood Heater reads its neighbours through one capability cache for each face, and the Railcraft blast furnace keeps its recipe by its input item. 8. Railcraft burns a boiler's fuel once in each cycle, at its fuel step, so the burn time that the workers of a boiler leave when they stop lasts at most one fuel step of the boiler, 16 ticks at low pressure and 8 at high pressure, not 2 ticks (CD-FUEL-14). | `docs/plans/2026-11-15-v1.2-new-roles-and-compat.md` |
| R86. Rulings (2026-11-16) of the coordinator, under the approver's delegation, on six findings of the review of Tasks 6 to 8, and on the same gap as finding 6 in the Charge Cell. 1. An explosion never destroys an Experience Collector or a Charge Cell: neither loot table has the condition `minecraft:survives_explosion`, by which a creeper destroyed 2 in 3 of them with their XP or FE (CD-XPC-13, CD-GEN-11). 2. For 6,000 ticks after a player dies, the Soul Collector counts the spot of the death as a player who is no spectator, so the orbs of a death wait for their player, who respawns elsewhere. The spots live in memory, bounded, and an expired one goes (CD-SOU-12). 3. The Soul Collector reads the players near its work area with one entity query per call and squared distances, keeps the takeable flag of each orb until its next query, and sorts the orbs nearest first once per query. 4. A grant of the collector's XP that leaves the player's total XP as it was, as when a listener cancels NeoForge's `PlayerXpEvent.XpChange`, puts the XP back into the store (CD-XPC-05). 5. A batch of the Soul Collector reads the capacity once and puts the XP into the collector before it changes the orb, and a take that the collector cuts changes the orb only by the XP that the collector took (CD-SOU-07). 6. A collector that holds XP and that a player in creative mode breaks drops its item with the XP, as a shulker box does (CD-XPC-13). 7. (2026-11-17) A Charge Cell that holds FE and that a player in creative mode breaks drops its item with the FE, as the collector of item 6 does (CD-GEN-13). | `docs/plans/2026-11-15-v1.2-new-roles-and-compat.md` |

| R88. Rulings (2026-11-17) of the coordinator, under the approver's delegation, on the review of Tasks 15 to 17. 1. Code that a mixin runs inside another mod's tick reads that mod only through shadows, or inside `try/catch (LinkageError)`, and a failure there turns its target off as the engine's guard does (ADR-0020). 2. The Egg Incubator respects its own `speedUpgradeEnabled` setting: where the server turns Speed Upgrades off, the worker gives no speed and the incubator has no demand. It also has no demand while an Analyse Upgrade holds its egg or its filter rejects the egg (CD-FUEL-11). 3. Unusual End's budding citrine grows only under a worker: its random tick does nothing in Minecraft 1.21.1, and its scheduled tick grows a face with no roll of its own. The Crystal Tender gives that tick on 1 extra tick in 5, the chance of budding amethyst, by the role file's `scheduled_tick_one_in`, and only air is an open face of such a block (CD-MCH-04). 4. The Liquid Blaze Burner has demand only while the worker's heat is above the heat of its own fuel. 5. The AE2 crank keeps `applyTurn()`, because `power()` would break the crank, so it charges its machine without turning on screen, and the player guide says so. | `docs/plans/2026-11-15-v1.2-new-roles-and-compat.md` |
| R89. Rulings (2026-11-17) of the coordinator, under the approver's delegation, on the detection of Dynamic Trees by the Lumberjack and on five look-alikes of cave vines. 1. A log or a branch of the namespace `dynamictrees` takes Dynamic Trees' own fell, whatever its tags, and so does a block that the Dynamic Trees hook reports as a `BranchBlock` while Dynamic Trees is loaded. With no hook, such a tree is no tree, and the handler never fells it itself. Without this rule, a rename of `#dynamictrees:branches` would let the handler remove the branches with `setBlock`, after which Dynamic Trees destroys the rest of the tree with no drops. This amends CD-LUM-25. 2. Five look-alikes of cave vines are growth-only: the Cultivator grows them, and they stay out of the Harvester's tag and of `harvest/cave_vines`. The block loot of `allthemodium:ancient_cavevines_plant`, `ars_elemental:source_vines` and `ars_elemental:source_vines_plant` drops no berry, and that of `yungscavebiomes:prickly_peach_cactus` drops the cactus itself, while each mod's own pick drops the berry or the peach. Deeper and Darker's vine tip `glowing_vines` has no `berries` property. Task 20 applies this ruling to the crop table of ADR-0020 and to the notes of CD-FRM-01 and CD-FRM-08. | `docs/plans/2026-11-15-v1.2-new-roles-and-compat.md` |

| R91. Ruling (2026-11-17) of the coordinator, under the approver's delegation, on the flake of the GameTest `cdTlw06Tlw09Tlw08TheBonusFollowsTheWork`: fix the product, not the order of the test. Cobblemon's idle hover can leave a Flying-type in the air when its claim starts. Its flight path to a spot under the sails of a windmill that faces up crosses the sails, which the pathfinder does not see, and it lands on them 1.63 blocks above its spot, beyond the 1.5 blocks of the arrival rule. The Tailwind handler counts a Traveling worker as arrived when it stands anywhere on its own windmill's contraption, the sails that it is bound to work, at or above the height of its spot. The engine's snap then moves the worker from the sails to its spot, which lies within the role's range of 6 blocks of the bearing. The rule stays in the Tailwind handler, through a general arrival hook of the engine at a flat cost per tick, and every other role keeps the 1.5-block rule. The test keeps its order, and a regression GameTest puts the bound Pidgey on the sail sheet, over a cell other than its spot, when its claim starts. Task 25 applies this ruling, which adds CD-TLW-11. Amended (2026-11-17) by the coordinator, under the approver's delegation: the first form of this ruling also asked that the worker stand within the arrival's horizontal reach of its spot. With that condition, the flake test still failed 1 time in 40, because Cobblemon's pathing held the Pidgey on the sails over another cell, more than 0.75 blocks from its spot, until the timeout of 600 ticks (CD-ASG-17). The amendment drops that condition. | `docs/plans/2026-11-15-v1.2-new-roles-and-compat.md` |
| R92. Ruling (2026-11-17) of the coordinator, under the approver's delegation, on audit (c) of Task 23 of the 1.2 plan: Dynamics logs a compat line only for a loaded mod that has a compat package, with adapters in code. A mod whose support is only tag entries and data-map entries logs no line. Its tag entries are optional, and each of its data-map entries carries its own condition, so they load or drop with the mod, and it has no adapter to name. About 16 such mods of the table of ADR-0016 had no line before this ruling, so the first wording of CD-OPT-11 was untrue for them. Apothic Enchanting has a compat package, so its line names its stats hook as the adapter `muse_quanta`. ADR-0016 records this choice in an amendment. | `docs/adr/0016-zero-setup-optional-mod-compatibility.md` |

Research: the existing roles on the machines of Immersive Engineering 12.4.2-194, Railcraft Reborn 1.2.17, Mekanism 10.7.19.85 with its Generators, AE2 19.2.19 and Botany Pots 21.1.44, read with `javap` and at the source tag of each jar on 2026-11-14. | `docs/adr/0020-machines-and-crops-of-other-mods.md` |
| RES11. Research: the audit of the COHO machines that each role does not reach, 95 rows, over the 461 jars of COHO's frozen cache of 2026-11-02, on 2026-11-15. | `docs/adr/0020-machines-and-crops-of-other-mods.md` |
| RES12. Research: the audit of popular NeoForge 1.21.1 mods outside COHO, 82 rows, read at their 1.21.1 sources and through the Modrinth API on 2026-11-15. | `docs/adr/0020-machines-and-crops-of-other-mods.md` |
| RES13. Research: the checks of the 1.2 design on 2026-11-15: `javap` of experiencelib 1.2.1, Applied Experienced 1.3.2, Mekanism 10.7.19.85 and Mekanism Generators 10.7.19.85, the Twilight Forest 4.8.3345 and the vanilla classes of the freeze, farmland, ice and menu rules; the block ids, mod ids and licenses of the COHO jars; and the versions and licenses of the Modrinth API. | `docs/adr/0016-zero-setup-optional-mod-compatibility.md` |

| BM1. Benchmark: Cobbleworkers 2.0.7. | https://modrinth.com/mod/cobbleworkers |
| BM2. Benchmark: Cobblebase 2.0.1. | https://modrinth.com/mod/cobblebase |
| BM3. Benchmark: a small 6 × 6 × 6 Extreme Reactors reactor gives about 15,000 FE/t at most, by the approver's figure. | none |
| BM4. Benchmark: the Mechanical Harvester of Create 6.0.10 (`HarvesterMovementBehaviour`) harvests each fully grown crop that its contraption passes, one crop per harvester per block of travel, and replants it by setting the crop's age to 0. | RES8 |
| BM5. Benchmark: a player who holds the use key with bone meal applies it every 4 ticks (`Minecraft.startUseItem` sets `rightClickDelay` to 4), which is 300 uses per minute, and a dispenser fires 4 ticks after its signal. | RES8 |
| BM6. Benchmark: a player with an iron axe breaks a log in 10 ticks (hardness 2, speed 6), and Oritech's Treefeller fells at most 1 log per tick. | RES9, RES12 |
| BM7. Benchmark: a player picks up one orb unit every 2 ticks (`takeXpDelay` 2). | RES9 |
| BM8. Benchmark: a cold biome tries 3 random columns in 48 per tick in each chunk at `randomTickSpeed` 3, about 75 per minute (`ServerLevel.tickChunk`). | RES13 |
| BM9. Benchmark: a Thermal Evaporation Plant of height 18 saturates at about 1,870 J/t of heat, a Fuelwood Heater makes 400 J/t, and a level 100 Generator through a Resistive Heater makes 7,500 J/t (derived from the code). | RES10 |
| BM10. Benchmark: one lava block gives a 300 K PneumaticCraft machine about 10 heat per tick, and an Advanced Air Compressor makes 50 mL of air per tick. | RES12 |
| BM11. Benchmark: an IE Windmill with 8 sails gives 35.6 FE/t in clear weather and 71.8 FE/t in a thunderstorm, and an optimized IE Watermill gives about 29 FE/t (unverified for 12.4.2). | RES10 |
| BM12. Benchmark: a Mekanism Wind Generator gives `windGenerationMin` (60 J/t) times its height multiplier, up to `windGenerationMax` (480 J/t). | RES13 |
| BM13. Benchmark: a Cobblemon Healing Machine recharges `maxHealerCharge` (6 on COHO) in `secondsToChargeHealingMachine` (900 s on COHO). | RES11 |
| BM14. Benchmark: at each fuel step, a Railcraft Reborn boiler of T tanks burns (8 − 0.1 × T + 0.8 × h + 4 × M ÷ 1,000) × T burn ticks (`SteamBoiler.getFuelPerCycle`), times its efficiency and Railcraft's `fuelPerSteamMultiplier`, both 1 by default. M, its top temperature, is 500 at low pressure and 1,000 at high pressure, h is its temperature ÷ M, and a fuel step comes every 16 ticks at low pressure and every 8 at high pressure. At the top temperature, 1 low-pressure tank over 1 firebox block needs 10.7 per step, 2 × 2 × 2 low-pressure tanks over 4 blocks need 80, 20 for each block, and 3 × 3 × 4 high-pressure tanks over 9 blocks need 331.2, about 41 per tick and 36.8 for each block. | RES10 |

## Assumptions

none: the approver ruled on every assumption on 2026-11-09. Inputs R35 to R57 hold those rulings.

## Role table

The rows below cite this table. It names each role's phase, types, targets, effect, auto-assign flag
and default priority. The default role files hold these values.

| Role | Phase | Types | Targets | Effect | Auto-assign | Priority |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Wheel Driver | 1 | Water | Create water wheel, large water wheel | Water Gun | yes | 0 |
| Generator | 1 | Electric | Blocks whose NeoForge energy capability can receive | Thunder Shock | yes | 0 |
| Stoker | 1 | Fire | Block entities that extend `AbstractFurnaceBlockEntity` | Ember | yes | 0 |
| Superheater | 1 | Dragon | Filled Create Blaze Burner | Dragon Breath | yes | 10 |
| Cranker | 1 | Fighting, Normal | Create hand crank | Pound | yes | 0 |
| Crystal Tender | 1 | Rock | Blocks in `#c:budding_blocks` | Rock Throw | yes | 0 |
| Brew Stoker | 1 | Fire | Brewing stand | Ember | yes | 10 |
| Brewer | 1 | Poison | Brewing stand | Poison Gas | yes | 0 |
| Pump | 1 | Water | Blocks whose NeoForge fluid capability accepts water, and cauldrons | Water Gun | no, leash only | 0 |
| Muse | 1 | Fairy | Enchanting table | Fairy Wind | no, leash only | 0 |
| healer | 1 | Fairy | Allies in the same pasture | Floral Healing if Cobblemon has it, otherwise the `special` pose with heal particles | not a machine role | none |
| Telekinesis | 2 | Psychic | A source and a destination that expose NeoForge's item handler capability | Kinesis | no, leash only | 0 |
| Magnet | 2 | Steel | A container that exposes NeoForge's item handler capability | `special` pose with `cobblemon:metal_sparks` | no, leash only | 0 |
| Fan Catalyst | 2 | Fire, Water, Ghost | Create encased fan | Fire Spin (Fire), Bubble (Water), Will-O-Wisp (Ghost) | no, leash only | 0 |
| Tailwind | 2 | Flying | Create windmill bearing | Gust | no, leash only | 0 |
| Cultivator | 1.1 | Ground | Crops in `#cobblemon_dynamics:cultivator_targets` that a crop adapter grows | Mud Sport | yes | 0 |
| Harvester | 1.1 | Grass | Fully grown crops in `#cobblemon_dynamics:harvester_targets` that a crop adapter harvests, never an apricorn | Razor Leaf | yes | 0 |
| Lumberjack | 1.2 | Bug | Natural trees whose base log is in `#cobblemon_dynamics:lumberjack_targets`, and saplings | Aerial Ace | no, leash only | 0 |
| Soul Collector | 1.2 | Ghost | Experience Collector | Night Shade | no, leash only | 0 |
| Freezer | 1.2 | Ice | Water sources under air | Ice Beam | yes, with no Quick Work | 0 |
| Heater | 1.2 | Fire | Mekanism heat valves (`heater_mekanism`), PneumaticCraft heat machines (`heater_pneumaticcraft`) | Ember | yes for Mekanism, no (leash only) for PneumaticCraft | 0 |
| The role files of other mods | 1.2 | The types of their role | The targets of ADR-0020 | The effect of their role | As their role | 0 |

## Recipes

| Item | Recipe |
| --- | --- |
| Work Leash | Shapeless: 1 Lead and 1 Poké Ball (`cobblemon:poke_ball`). |
| Hay Bed | Shaped: 3 hay bales in a row over 3 planks of any wood. |
| Wool Bed | Shaped: a Hay Bed in the center, 4 wool blocks (`#minecraft:wool`) on the edges, 4 iron ingots in the corners. |
| Plush Bed | Shaped: a Wool Bed in the center, 4 gold ingots on the edges, 4 diamonds in the corners. |
| Starlit Bed | Shapeless: 1 Plush Bed, 1 Nether Star and 4 Rare Candy (`cobblemon:rare_candy`). |
| Charge Cell | Shaped: a Thunder Stone (`cobblemon:thunder_stone`) in the center, redstone dust above and below it, and 6 copper ingots in the side columns. |
| Experience Collector | Shaped: iron ingots in the 4 corners, glass in the top middle and on both sides of the middle row, a block of lapis lazuli in the centre, and a hopper in the bottom middle. |

## Milestones

| Milestone | Phase | Scope |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M0 | 1 | Scaffold and CI |
| M1 | 1 | Work engine, claims, Work Leash, Work Mode and the Stoker role |
| M2 | 1 | Work Stamina, beds and healers |
| M3 | 1 | Create roles: Wheel Driver, Superheater and Cranker |
| M4 | 1 | Generator, Crystal Tender, Brew Stoker, Brewer, Muse and Pump |
| M5 | 1 | UI bars, compatibility and polish. Ends at the Phase 1 gate. |
| M6 | 2 | Telekinesis and Magnet |
| M7 | 2 | Fan Catalyst and Tailwind |
| QW | 2 | Quick Work for party Cobblemon. One pull request, which merges before M8 Task 6. |
| M8 | 2 | Final art and the approver's art review. Ends at the Phase 2 gate. |
| M9 | 3 | Land claims for pastures, MineColonies and the real claim mod tests, then the public release 1.0.0 and the approver's pack. The [Phase 3 plan](2026-11-11-phase-3-release.md) holds it. |
| M10 | 1.1 | The Cultivator, the Harvester and the crop adapters for vanilla crops and the crop mods of COHO, then the release 1.1.0. The [1.1 plan](2026-11-12-v1.1-farming.md) holds it. |
| M11 | 1.2 | The fixes, the Lumberjack, the Experience Collector, the Soul Collector, the Freezer, the Heater, and the machines and crops of other mods with no setup, then the release 1.2.0. The [1.2 plan](2026-11-15-v1.2-new-roles-and-compat.md) holds it. |

## Requirements

### PLAT: platform, dependencies and versions

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-PLAT-01 | The mod MUST declare NeoForge `[21.1.219,)` as a required dependency. | MUST | R7, RES2, RES3, ADR 0002 | Create 6.0.10 needs NeoForge 21.1.219, and attachment sync needs 21.1.197. | Demo: NeoForge 21.1.219 loads the mod, and 21.1.218 shows the dependency error screen. Fails if: 21.1.219 fails to load, or 21.1.218 loads. | build | agreed |
| CD-PLAT-02 | The mod MUST declare Cobblemon `[1.8.4,1.9)` as a required dependency. | MUST | R24, RES1 | Pasture and Brain internals change between Cobblemon minor versions. | Inspect: `neoforge.mods.toml` declares `[1.8.4,1.9)`. Demo: Cobblemon 1.8.4 loads the mod, and 1.8.3 shows the dependency error screen. Fails if: the range differs, 1.8.4 fails, or 1.8.3 loads. | build | agreed |
| CD-PLAT-03 | The mod MUST declare KotlinForForge `[5.10,)` as its language loader range. | MUST | R24, RES1, RES3 | Cobblemon 1.8.4 needs 5.10, and a higher floor locks out packs that Cobblemon supports. | Inspect: `neoforge.mods.toml` has `modLoader = "kotlinforforge"` and `loaderVersion = "[5.10,)"`. Demo: KotlinForForge 5.10.0 loads the mod. Fails if: either value differs, or 5.10.0 fails. | build | agreed |
| CD-PLAT-04 | The build MUST compile every class to Java 21 bytecode. | MUST | R24, RES1 | Cobblemon runs only on Java 21. | Inspect: `javap -v` on each class in the jar reports major version 65. Fails if: a class reports another version. | build | agreed |
| CD-PLAT-05 | The mod MUST declare Create `[6.0.10,6.1)` and AE2 `[19.2,20)` as optional dependencies. | MUST | R7, R9, RES2, RES3, ADR 0002 | Packs without Create or AE2 still get the vanilla and FE roles. | Inspect: both entries have `type = "optional"` and these ranges. Demo: a pack with Create 6.0.9 shows the dependency error screen, a pack with AE2 19.1.x shows it too, and a pack with neither mod loads. Fails if: either mod is required, a range differs, 6.0.9 loads, or AE2 19.1.x loads. | build | agreed |
| CD-PLAT-06 | The mod MUST require an install on both the client and the server. | MUST | R22, R24 | The mod adds items, blocks and screens that a client without the mod cannot show. | Demo: a client without the mod joins a server with it and gets NeoForge's missing-mod message. Fails if: the client joins. | build | agreed |
| CD-PLAT-07 | The mod MUST use the mod id `cobblemon_dynamics` and the Maven coordinates `codes.honor:cobblemon-dynamics`. | MUST | R48, R9 | Datapacks, configs and other mods address the mod by these names. | Inspect: `neoforge.mods.toml` and the published POM. Fails if: either name differs. | build | agreed |
| CD-PLAT-08 | The mod MUST NOT declare Cobbleworkers or Cobblebase as a required dependency. | MUST NOT | ADR 0003, R20, RES4 | Dynamics is standalone, and both mods are optional neighbors. | Inspect: `neoforge.mods.toml` lists neither mod as required. Fails if: either appears as required. | build | agreed |

### WMD: Work Mode

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-WMD-01 | When a player places a pasture, the pasture adapter MUST set Work Mode to off. | MUST | R1, ADR 0001 | Pastures keep Cobblemon's behaviour until someone opts in. | GameTest: place a pasture and read its Work Mode attachment. Fails if: Work Mode reads on. | pasture adapter | agreed |
| CD-WMD-02 | The pasture adapter MUST save Work Mode with Cobblemon's pasture block entity. | MUST | R1, RES1, RES3 | A restart must not stop a base. | GameTest: set Work Mode on, then unload and reload the chunk. Fails if: Work Mode reads off after the reload. | pasture adapter | agreed |
| CD-WMD-03 | When the pasture's placer sends a Work Mode request, the server MUST set Work Mode to the requested state. | MUST | R1, R24, ADR 0001 | The placer controls the pasture. | GameTest: the placer requests on, then off. Fails if: Work Mode does not follow each request within 1 tick. | pasture adapter | agreed |
| CD-WMD-04 | When an operator sends a Work Mode request for any pasture, the server MUST set Work Mode to the requested state. | MUST | R24, R39 | Operators fix abandoned or misused pastures. | GameTest: a permission-level-2 player who is not the placer requests on. Fails if: Work Mode stays off. | pasture adapter | agreed |
| CD-WMD-05 | When Work Mode turns off, the claim registry MUST release every work, bed and heal claim of that pasture's workers within 1 tick. | MUST | R1, R24, REV1, state × event sweep | Workers of a pasture that is off must free machines, beds and allies for others. Leash bindings stay, as CD-CLM-06 says. | GameTest: two Working workers hold work claims, a sleeper holds a bed claim, and a healer holds a heal claim. Turn Work Mode off and read the registry 1 tick later. Fails if: any of the four claims remains. | claim registry | agreed |
| CD-WMD-06 | While Work Mode is off, the work engine MUST NOT give any claim to a Cobblemon of that pasture. | MUST NOT | R1 | Off means Cobblemon's normal behaviour. | GameTest: Work Mode off, and a Fire-type beside a furnace with input for 400 ticks. Fails if: the Fire-type gets a claim. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-WMD-07 | When a pasture block is broken, the work engine MUST clear the leash bindings of every Cobblemon that was tethered to it. | MUST | R24, entity lifecycle sweep | Cobblemon sends those Cobblemon back to the PC, so their bindings point nowhere. | GameTest: bind two workers, then break their pasture. Fails if: either binding remains 20 ticks later. | work engine | agreed |

### ASG: assignment and worker states

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-ASG-01 | The work engine MUST give claims only to owned, unfainted Cobblemon that are tethered to a pasture in Work Mode or that hold a Quick Work order (CD-QWK-02). | MUST | R2, G4, R63, actor × action sweep | Wild and fainted Cobblemon never work, and a party Cobblemon works only on a Quick Work order. | GameTest: a wild Water-type, a party Water-type that a player sent out with no Quick Work aim, a fainted pastured Water-type and a healthy pastured Water-type stand 3 blocks from a dry water wheel for 400 ticks. Fails if: any Cobblemon except the healthy pastured one gets a claim. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-02 | The role registry MUST count a Cobblemon as eligible for a role when its primary or secondary type is one of the role's types. | MUST | R15, R24 | Dual types fill either role, for example a Water/Flying Gyarados drives wheels. | Unit: Gyarados (Water/Flying) is eligible for the Wheel Driver, and Pikachu (Electric) is not. Fails if: the secondary type is ignored, or an unlisted type qualifies. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-ASG-03 | When the work engine scans a pasture in Work Mode, it MUST give each Idle eligible worker the nearest unclaimed target of an auto-assign role that the worker can do. | MUST | R1, R24, G1 | Workers find work with no player input. | GameTest: furnaces with input at 3 and 6 blocks from one Idle Fire-type. Fails if: the worker claims the 6-block furnace, or no furnace. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-04 | The work engine MUST NOT auto-assign a Cobblemon with a Fairy type to a machine. | MUST NOT | R18, R24 | Fairy-types heal unless a player leashes them. | GameTest: an unleashed Azumarill (Water/Fairy) stands 3 blocks from a dry water wheel for 400 ticks. Fails if: it claims the wheel. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-05 | The work engine MUST NOT auto-assign a worker to a role whose auto-assign flag is false. | MUST NOT | R18, R19, R29 | Pumps, Muses and the four Phase 2 roles act only where a player binds them. | GameTest: an unleashed Water-type stands beside an empty fluid tank for 400 ticks. Fails if: it claims the tank. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-06 | With the default config, the work engine MUST start each new scan pass of a pasture in Work Mode at the first 40-tick boundary, counted from the previous pass's start, after that pass completes. | MUST | R24, REV1, limits sweep | Scans find new machines without a cost on every tick, and a pass that overruns 40 ticks must finish before the next one starts. | Unit: on a fake clock with the default config, passes start at ticks 0, 40 and 80, and none starts at tick 39. A pass that needs 108 ticks (radius 32 × 6 with a Rock-type worker) is followed by a pass at tick 120. Fails if: a pass starts before the previous pass completes, or later than the first 40-tick boundary after it. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-07 | With the default config, the work engine MUST check at most 512 block positions per pasture per tick during a scan. | MUST | R24, limits sweep, quality sweep | A large work area must not cause a lag spike. | Unit: with a Rock-type worker in the pasture, a scan of the default 17 × 13 × 17 area (3,757 positions) runs over 8 ticks, and no tick checks a 513th position. Fails if: a tick checks more than 512 positions. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-08 | The work engine MUST limit the work area to block offsets of at most 8 horizontally and 6 vertically from the pasture block, with the default config. | MUST | R24, limits sweep | A small work area keeps pasture placement strategic. | GameTest: furnaces with input at horizontal offsets 8 and 9, and at vertical offsets 6 and 7. Fails if: a worker claims the furnace at offset 9 or 7, or never claims the one at offset 8 or 6. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-09 | When a worker gets a claim, the work engine MUST pick a standable work spot inside the work area that has line of sight to the target center and does not stand on the target, preferring a spot beside the target at its base height, then another spot at that height, then a spot at another height, and taking a spot that a worker cannot walk into on the level, such as a pit, only when no other spot exists. | MUST | R24, RES1 | A worker that cannot see its target cannot aim its move at it. A worker beside its target, on the floor that the target stands on, looks like it works the target, and a worker on top of the target does not (approver's play-test, 2026-11-10). | Unit: in a test grid with a wall between one candidate spot and the target, the chosen spot has a clear line to the target center. A spot beside the target wins over spots nearer to the worker, also beside the footprint of a multi-block target, a spot on the target is never chosen, and a pit beside the target comes after every open spot. GameTest: a Charmander gets a spot beside a furnace on the floor, at floor height, and a Stoker works its furnace from the one spot beside it. Fails if: the chosen spot is blocked, outside the work area, not standable or on the target, or another spot is chosen while a free, open spot beside the target at its base height has room for the worker, or a closed spot is chosen while an open spot exists. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-10 | With the default role files, the work engine MUST pick work spots within 6 blocks of the target center for ranged roles and within 2 blocks for the Cranker. | MUST | R24, limits sweep | The range sets how far a move reaches. | Unit: a spot at 6.0 blocks is valid for the Stoker and one at 6.1 is not. A spot at 2.0 blocks is valid for the Cranker and one at 2.1 is not. Fails if: any of the four results is wrong. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-11 | When a worker reaches its work spot while the target has demand, the work engine MUST put the worker in Working. | MUST | R24, state × event sweep | Work starts on arrival. | GameTest: a Fire-type reaches a furnace with input. Fails if: it is not Working within 1 tick of arrival. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-12 | While a worker is at its work spot and the target has no demand, the work engine MUST keep the worker in Standby with its claim. An auto-assigned farming worker releases its claim instead (CD-CRP-10). | MUST | R24, R45 | The worker stays ready and keeps its machine. | GameTest: a furnace runs out of input, then gets new input. Fails if: the Stoker releases its claim, or it is not Working within 20 ticks of the new input. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-13 | With the default config, if an auto-assigned worker has not reached its work spot 600 ticks (30 s) after its work claim, then the claim registry MUST release that claim. | MUST | R24, REV1, failure sweep, limits sweep | An unreachable target must not hold a worker forever. | GameTest: a target walled off from every spot. Fails if: the claim is not held at tick 599 after the claim, or is still held at tick 601. | claim registry | agreed |
| CD-ASG-14 | With the default config, when an auto-assigned worker's travel times out, the work engine MUST NOT give that worker the same target for 6,000 ticks (5 min). | MUST NOT | R24, REV1, limits sweep | Repeated timeouts on one target waste every scan. | Unit: on a fake clock, the target is refused to that worker 5,999 ticks after the timeout and allowed at 6,000 ticks. Fails if: either result is wrong. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-15 | When a worker leaves its pasture, the claim registry MUST release the worker's claim within 20 ticks. | MUST | R24, RES1, failure sweep | Cobblemon fires no unpasture event, so the pasture adapter polls every 20 ticks. | GameTest: move a Working worker to the PC and read the registry 20 ticks later. Fails if: the claim remains. | claim registry | agreed |
| CD-ASG-16 | When a worker qualifies for two roles on one target, the work engine MUST give it the role whose role file has the higher `priority`. | MUST | R24, R42, REV1 | One worker per target means one role per target, and the stronger role wins. | Unit: with the default role files, a Poison/Fire Salazzle on a brewing stand gets the Brew Stoker role (priority 10 over 0), and a Fire/Dragon Turtonator on a Blaze Burner gets the Superheater role, the burner's only role. Fails if: either gets another role. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-ASG-17 | With the default config, if a leashed worker has not reached its work spot 600 ticks after its work claim, then the work engine MUST keep its leash binding and hold it in Standby for 6,000 ticks before it travels again. | MUST | R24, REV1, failure sweep, limits sweep | A leashed machine stays reserved for its own worker, and the worker tries again later. | Unit: on a fake clock, the binding stays, the worker is in Standby from tick 600 to tick 6,599 after the claim, and it is Traveling again at tick 6,600. Fails if: the binding clears, or the retry starts at another tick. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-18 | The work engine MUST keep each worker in exactly one of the states Idle, Traveling, Working, Standby, ToBed, Sleeping, RestingInPlace and Healing. | MUST | REV1, state × event sweep | One state list drives the icons, the claims and the stamina rules. | Unit: run a scripted day of 24,000 ticks for a worker and a healer, and record the state set on every tick. Fails if: a tick has no state, two states, or a state outside the list. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-19 | With the default config, when a scan finds an unclaimed target with demand for one of an auto-assigned worker's roles in its work area, and that worker has been in Standby for 200 ticks or more, the work engine MUST move the worker's work claim to that target. An auto-assigned farming worker never waits in Standby, because it releases its claim (CD-CRP-10). | MUST | R43, R44, limits sweep, state × event sweep | A worker must not wait at a machine with no work while another machine needs it, for example at a wheel that real water already turns. | Unit: on a fake clock, a Stoker in Standby at an empty furnace sees a second furnace with input. A scan at Standby tick 199 leaves its claim, and a scan at tick 200 moves it to the second furnace. GameTest: a Wheel Driver that has waited 400 ticks at a wheel that real water turns moves to a dry wheel in its work area within 80 ticks of the dry wheel's placement. Fails if: the claim moves at tick 199, it stays at tick 200, or the Wheel Driver does not move. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-ASG-20 | The work engine MUST NOT move a leashed worker off its bound target. | MUST NOT | R44, state × event sweep | A leash is the player's exact assignment. | GameTest: a leashed Stoker waits in Standby at an empty furnace for 400 ticks, while an unclaimed furnace with input sits 3 blocks away. Fails if: the leashed worker moves, claims the second furnace, or loses its binding. | work engine | agreed |

### CLM: claims

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-CLM-01 | The claim registry MUST hold at most one exclusive claim for each target. | MUST | R2, G4, ADR 0005, R66 | Two workers on one machine break the one-worker rule. The work claims of logistics roles are shared claims instead (CD-CLM-10). | Unit: workers from two pastures with different owners claim one furnace in the same tick. Fails if: both hold a claim. | claim registry | agreed |
| CD-CLM-02 | The claim registry MUST hold at most one claim (a work, bed or heal claim) for each worker. | MUST | R2, R24, REV1, ADR 0005 | One worker does one job at a time. | Unit: a worker with a work claim asks for a second target, and a sleeper with a bed claim asks for a heal claim. Fails if: the registry grants either second claim. | claim registry | agreed |
| CD-CLM-03 | The claim registry MUST key block targets by dimension and block position. | MUST | RES4, BM1, failure sweep | Cobbleworkers keys claims without the dimension, so equal coordinates in two dimensions collide. | Unit: claims on 0 64 0 in the overworld and on 0 64 0 in the Nether. Fails if: the registry refuses the second claim. | claim registry | agreed |
| CD-CLM-04 | The claim registry MUST store a multiblock target under its canonical target. | MUST | R2, RES2, ADR 0005 | A large water wheel has nine blocks but is one machine. | GameTest: worker A claims a large wheel through one ring block, and worker B tries another ring block of the same wheel. Fails if: worker B gets a claim. | claim registry | agreed |
| CD-CLM-05 | When a player makes a leash binding of an exclusive role on a target that an auto-assigned worker holds, the claim registry MUST release that worker's exclusive work claim within 1 tick. | MUST | R3, R24, G5, REV1, R66 | The leash is the player's override. A binding of a logistics role takes no claim from another worker (CD-CLM-11). | GameTest: worker A holds a work claim on a furnace, and the player binds worker B to it. Fails if: worker A still holds the claim 1 tick later, or worker B holds no work claim on the furnace 20 ticks later. | claim registry | agreed |
| CD-CLM-06 | The claim registry MUST NOT give an exclusive work claim on a target that a leash binding of an exclusive role reserves to any worker except the bound one. | MUST NOT | R3, R24, REV1, R66 | A leashed machine waits for its own worker, also while that worker sleeps or its pasture's Work Mode is off. A binding of a logistics role reserves no target, and it takes a place at its container instead (CD-LSH-29). | GameTest: the bound worker sleeps, and an Idle worker of the same type stands 3 blocks from the target for 400 ticks.
