The destination follows CD-TLK-12. | GameTest: with the default config, a source at horizontal offset 9 and one at offset 8, each with a destination inside the work area. Fails if: the offset-9 bind succeeds, or the offset-8 bind fails. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-TLK-10 | If the destination is the source block, then the work engine MUST refuse the bind with `"Destination must differ from the source"`. | MUST | R57, misuse sweep | A loop on one block moves nothing and wastes a worker. | GameTest: click the same chest as source and as destination. Fails if: a binding exists, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-TLK-11 | When a Telekinesis source or destination is broken or replaced, the work engine MUST clear the binding. | MUST | entity lifecycle sweep | A binding to a missing container moves nothing. | GameTest: break the destination of one binding, and replace the source of a second binding with stone. Fails if: either binding remains 20 ticks later. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-TLK-12 | If the Telekinesis destination sits more than 32 blocks from the pasture block on any axis, then the work engine MUST refuse the bind with `"Too far from its pasture"`. | MUST | R54, R24, limits sweep | A destination may sit outside the work area, but not past Cobblemon's default roam box. | GameTest: with the default Telekinesis role file, destinations at offsets 32 and 33 from the pasture block on the x axis. Fails if: the offset-32 bind fails, the offset-33 bind succeeds, or the refusal shows another message. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-TLK-13 | The Telekinesis handler MUST NOT lose or duplicate items while several workers move items from one source at once. | MUST NOT | R66, RES5, failure sweep | Two workers that take from one container in one tick each see what the other left, because every move runs on the server thread through one batch. | GameTest: two Psychic-types bind one chest of 256 items as their source, one to barrel A and one to barrel B, and both Work. Fails if: a worker never Works, the two never Work in the same tick, either barrel gets no item, or at any tick the items in the chest, in both barrels and on the ground add up to anything but 256. | target handler (NeoForge caps) | agreed |

### MAG: Magnet (Phase 2)

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-MAG-01 | The role registry MUST enable the Magnet role for the Steel type, on blocks that expose NeoForge's item handler capability. | MUST | R16, R31, R33, G3 | Steel-types collect drops into a container. | GameTest: bind a Steel-type to a chest. Fails if: the bind is refused. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-MAG-02 | The role registry MUST set the Magnet role's auto-assign flag to false. | MUST | R29 | The approver ruled the Phase 2 roles leash only. | Inspect: the Magnet role file has `"auto_assign": false`. Fails if: the flag is true or missing. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-MAG-03 | With the default Magnet role file, the handler MUST insert at most ceil(64 × L ÷ 100) dropped items into its container in each 20-tick batch. | MUST | R31, R52, limits sweep | The approver set the rate. | Unit: with 200 items on the ground, Lv1 inserts 1, Lv50 inserts 32 and Lv100 inserts 64 per batch. Fails if: a batch inserts more, or fewer while the container has room. | target handler (NeoForge caps) | agreed |
| CD-MAG-04 | The Magnet handler MUST collect only dropped items inside its worker's pasture work area. | MUST | R31, limits sweep | The approver set the range to the whole work area. | GameTest: with the default config, items at horizontal offsets 8 and 9 from the pasture block. Fails if: the offset-9 item is taken, or the offset-8 item is not. | target handler (NeoForge caps) | agreed |
| CD-MAG-05 | The Magnet handler MUST NOT collect an item that still has a pickup delay. | MUST NOT | R33, RES5 | A fresh drop belongs to the player who dropped it. | GameTest: a player drops an item with a 40-tick pickup delay in range. Fails if: the Magnet takes it before its delay ends, or has not taken it 40 ticks after the delay ends. | target handler (NeoForge caps) | agreed |
| CD-MAG-06 | The Magnet handler MUST NOT lose or duplicate items. | MUST NOT | RES5, failure sweep | A partial insert must leave the rest on the ground. | GameTest: a container with room for 10 items, and a stack of 64 on the ground, with a Lv100 Magnet. Fails if: the container gains more than 10 items, or the ground stack does not hold 54 after the batch. | target handler (NeoForge caps) | agreed |
| CD-MAG-07 | The Magnet handler MUST report demand only while a dropped item is in range and the container accepts it. | MUST | R33 | A Magnet with nothing to collect rests. | GameTest: no items on the ground. Then a full container with items on the ground. Fails if: the worker is not in Standby within 20 ticks in either case. | target handler (NeoForge caps) | agreed |
| CD-MAG-08 | The Magnet handler MUST look for dropped items at most once every 20 ticks. | MUST | RES5, quality sweep | An entity query on every tick costs server time. | Unit: on a fake clock, queries run at ticks 0, 20 and 40, and none runs at tick 19. Fails if: a query runs more often. | target handler (NeoForge caps) | agreed |
| CD-MAG-09 | The Magnet handler MUST collect into a container that is also a Telekinesis source while the Telekinesis worker moves items out of it, with no item lost or made. | MUST | R66, R31, R27 | The approver's flow: a Steel-type collects ground loot into a chest, and a Psychic-type moves it from the same chest to another container. | GameTest: 256 items lie on the ground in the work area. A Steel-type Magnet binds chest A, and a Psychic-type binds chest A as its source and chest B as its destination. Fails if: a bind is refused, the two workers never Work in the same tick, the items on the ground, in chest A and in chest B ever add up to anything but 256, or chest B does not end with all 256. | target handler (NeoForge caps) | agreed |

### FAN: Fan Catalyst (Phase 2)

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-FAN-01 | Where Create is present, the role registry MUST enable the Fan Catalyst role for Create encased fans, for the Fire, Water and Ghost types. | MUST | R16, R33, RES5, G3 | The worker replaces the lava, water or soul fire in front of a fan. | Unit: Charmander, Squirtle and Gastly are eligible, and Pikachu is not. Fails if: any result is wrong. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-FAN-02 | The role registry MUST set the Fan Catalyst role's auto-assign flag to false. | MUST | R29 | The approver ruled the Phase 2 roles leash only. | Inspect: the Fan Catalyst role file has `"auto_assign": false`. Fails if: the flag is true or missing. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-FAN-03 | The work engine MUST put a Fan Catalyst's work spot in the block directly in front of the fan's face. | MUST | R33, RES5 | Create checks the blocks along the air current, and a catalyst in the first block covers the whole line. | GameTest: bind a Charmander to a fan that faces east. Fails if: its work spot is not the block east of the fan. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-FAN-04 | If the block in front of the fan cannot hold the worker, then the work engine MUST refuse the bind with `"No room in front of the fan"`. | MUST | R57, failure sweep | A worker cannot stand inside a solid block. | GameTest: place stone in front of the fan, then bind. Fails if: a binding exists, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-FAN-05 | While a Fire-type Fan Catalyst works a fan in blasting mode, the fan MUST process items in its air current as Create's bulk blasting does. | MUST | R28, RES5 | Fire-types replace lava. | GameTest: raw iron on a belt in the air current. Fails if: it does not become an iron ingot. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-06 | While a Fire-type Fan Catalyst works a fan in smoking mode, the fan MUST process items in its air current as Create's bulk smoking does. | MUST | R28, RES5 | Fire-types replace a campfire for food. | GameTest: raw beef and raw iron in the air current. Fails if: the beef does not become steak, or the raw iron changes. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-07 | While a Water-type Fan Catalyst works a fan, the fan MUST process items in its air current as Create's bulk washing does. | MUST | R33, RES5 | Water-types replace a water source. | GameTest: red wool in the air current. Fails if: it does not become white wool. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-08 | While a Ghost-type Fan Catalyst works a fan, the fan MUST process items in its air current as Create's bulk haunting does. | MUST | R33, RES5 | Ghost-types replace soul fire. | GameTest: an item with a Create haunting recipe in the air current. Fails if: it does not become the recipe's output. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-09 | The Fan Catalyst handler MUST store a mode for each fan, starting at blasting. | MUST | R28 | Each fan keeps its own Fire mode. | GameTest: read the mode of a new fan. Fails if: it reads anything but blasting. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-10 | When the binder, the placer or an operator sneak-right-clicks a driven fan with the Work Leash, the Fan Catalyst handler MUST switch that fan's mode between blasting and smoking. | MUST | R28, REV1 | The approver ruled a per-fan flip, with the same gesture as a wheel. | GameTest: the binder flips a worked fan twice. Fails if: the mode does not read smoking after the first click and blasting after the second. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-11 | The Fan Catalyst handler MUST save each fan's mode with the fan. | MUST | failure sweep | A restart must not change a production line. | GameTest: flip a fan to smoking, then restart. Fails if: it reads blasting after the restart. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-12 | The Fan Catalyst handler MUST exempt its own worker from the fan's push and from the fan's effects on entities. | MUST | R33, RES5 | Create's fan pushes, burns, damages and blinds the entities in its current. | GameTest: a Fire, a Water and a Ghost worker each work a fan at 256 RPM for 1,200 ticks. Fails if: a worker moves more than 0.5 blocks from its spot, takes damage, burns, or gains blindness or slowness. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-13 | Dynamics MUST register its fan processing types with a priority above 400. | MUST | RES5 | A worker that stands in water must still count as its own catalyst, ahead of Create's washing at priority 400. | GameTest: a Ghost worker stands in a water source block in front of the fan. Fails if: the items are washed and not haunted. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-14 | While a Fan Catalyst is Working, the client MUST show the processing particles of its catalyst type in the air current. | MUST | RES5 | The client rebuilds the air current itself, so it must see the worker as a catalyst. | Demo: watch a fan with each of the three catalyst types. Fails if: the particles do not match the type. | client | agreed |
| CD-FAN-15 | The Fan Catalyst handler MUST report demand only while the fan spins and its air current holds at least one item. | MUST | R33, R45 | A fan with nothing to process needs no catalyst. | GameTest: stop the fan. Then restart it and remove all items from the current. Fails if: the worker is not in Standby within 20 ticks in either case. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-FAN-16 | When a fan's mode changes, the client MUST show `"Fan mode: Blasting"` or `"Fan mode: Smoking"` in the action bar. | MUST | R57 | The mode is not visible on the fan. | Demo: flip a fan twice. Fails if: the action bar does not show each new mode. | client | agreed |
| CD-FAN-17 | If a fan mixin fails to apply, then the role registry MUST disable the Fan Catalyst role and log one warning. | MUST | failure sweep | A Create update must not crash the game. | Unit: start with the mixin's applied flag set to false. Fails if: the role stays enabled, or the game crashes. | role registry | agreed |

### TLW: Tailwind (Phase 2)

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-TLW-01 | Where Create is present, the role registry MUST enable the Tailwind role for Create windmill bearings, for the Flying type. | MUST | R16, R33, G3 | Flying-types add wind to a windmill. | Unit: Pidgey is eligible, and Pikachu is not. Fails if: either result is wrong. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-TLW-02 | The role registry MUST set the Tailwind role's auto-assign flag to false. | MUST | R29 | The approver ruled the Phase 2 roles leash only. | Inspect: the Tailwind role file has `"auto_assign": false`. Fails if: the flag is true or missing. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-TLW-03 | With the default Tailwind role file, while a Tailwind worker is Working, the handler MUST add floor(S × L ÷ 100) virtual sails to a windmill with S real sails. | MUST | R30, R25 | Building sails stays the main lever, and levels help. | Unit: S = 64 at Lv50 adds 32 (96 sails, 12 RPM), S = 8 at Lv100 adds 8 (16 sails, 2 RPM), and S = 64 at Lv100 adds 64 (128 sails, 16 RPM). Fails if: a value differs. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-TLW-04 | The Tailwind handler MUST cap a windmill's real and virtual sails at 128 in total. | MUST | R30, limits sweep | Create caps a windmill at 16 RPM, which 128 sails reach. | Unit: S = 100 at Lv100 counts 128 sails, and S = 96 at Lv33 counts 127 sails. Fails if: either count differs. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-TLW-05 | The Tailwind handler MUST NOT count virtual sails toward the 8 real sails that a windmill needs to assemble. | MUST NOT | RES5 | Virtual sails add speed, not structure. | GameTest: a bearing with 7 real sails and a bound Lv100 Tailwind worker, and a player tries to assemble it. Fails if: the windmill assembles. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-TLW-06 | When a windmill's virtual sail bonus changes, the Tailwind handler MUST call `updateGeneratedRotation()` on the server within 1 tick. | MUST | RES5 | Create reads the speed live, but the network updates only on that call. | GameTest: a Lv50 worker starts Working on a 64-sail windmill. Fails if: the network does not turn at 12 RPM 2 ticks later. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-TLW-07 | The Tailwind handler MUST sync each windmill's virtual sail bonus to clients. | MUST | RES5 | Otherwise the client spins the windmill at the wrong rate and snaps it back every 3 ticks. | Demo: watch a worked windmill for 600 ticks. Fails if: its sails visibly snap. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-TLW-08 | The Tailwind handler MUST report demand only while the windmill is assembled and turning. | MUST | R33 | A stopped windmill needs no wind. | GameTest: disassemble the windmill. Fails if: the worker is not in Standby within 20 ticks. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-TLW-09 | When a Tailwind worker stops Working, the handler MUST remove its virtual sails within 2 ticks. | MUST | R24 | The bonus ends with the work. | GameTest: release the claim on a worked 64-sail windmill. Fails if: it does not turn at 8 RPM 2 ticks later. | target handler (Create) | agreed |
| CD-TLW-10 | If the windmill mixin fails to apply, then the role registry MUST disable the Tailwind role and log one warning. | MUST | failure sweep | A Create update must not crash the game. | Unit: start with the mixin's applied flag set to false. Fails if: the role stays enabled, or the game crashes. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-TLW-11 | When a Traveling Tailwind worker stands anywhere on the sails of the windmill that it travels to, at or above the height of its work spot, the Tailwind handler MUST count the worker as arrived. | MUST | R91 | The sails are a contraption, which the pathfinder does not see, so a worker that flies in can land on them and never reach the spot below. On the sails, its path can hold it over another cell. On arrival, the engine moves the worker from the sails to its spot. Amended on 2026-11-17 (R91): the row no longer asks that the worker stand within the arrival's horizontal reach of its spot. | GameTest: a bound Pidgey stands on the sail sheet of a 64-sail windmill, over the cell 2 blocks east of the bearing, when its claim starts. Its spot is the cell east of the bearing, 1 block away, beyond the arrival's horizontal reach. Fails if: the Pidgey is not Working on its spot within 20 ticks of the bind. | target handler (Create) | agreed |

### QWK: Quick Work (Phase 2)

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-QWK-01 | When a player sends out a party Cobblemon outside a battle, the work engine MUST trace the player's view at the send-out on the server, from the player's eye position along the server's copy of the player's rotation, and keep the first block that the trace hits within the trace range of CD-CFG-18 as the aimed block. | MUST | R63, ADR 0013 | The approver's request: the crosshair picks the target. The server's copy of the rotation needs no client change (ruling 1). Cobblemon ends the send-out 30 ticks after the key press, so the aim counts at the press. | GameTest: a player 10 blocks from furnace A looks at it, sends out a Charmander through Cobblemon's send-out handler, and looks at furnace B on the next tick. Fails if: the Charmander gets no Quick Work order on furnace A, or furnace B is claimed. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-02 | When the aimed block fits a role of CD-QWK-03 and no check of CD-QWK-04 to CD-QWK-10 stops the order, the work engine MUST start a Quick Work order: the Cobblemon takes a work claim on the role's canonical block and works it as a pasture worker does (CD-ASG-09, CD-ASG-11, CD-ASG-12, CD-STA-06, CD-FX-01). | MUST | R63, ADR 0013 | The approver: "charmander should attempt to power it". The order reuses the claims, the worker states, the Brain bridge, the handlers, the effects and Work Stamina (ruling 4). | GameTest: the scene of CD-QWK-01, with 8 raw iron in furnace A. Fails if: the Charmander holds no work claim on furnace A within 100 ticks of the send-out, is not Working within 400 ticks, furnace A is not lit while it works, or its Work Stamina does not fall while it works. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-03 | The work engine MUST pick the role of a Quick Work order among the roles that fit the Cobblemon's types and the aimed block, whose auto-assign flag is true, whose handler a pasture scan visits and that name one target, as an auto claim picks it: the higher priority, then the role of the primary type among roles of one handler, then the role id that sorts first. | MUST | R63, R42, R55 | Quick Work never takes a role that needs the Work Leash (ruling 2), and the order gets the role that a pasture worker gets on the same block (CD-ASG-16, CD-LSH-16). | Unit: with the default role files, a Poison/Fire Salazzle on a brewing stand gets the Brew Stoker, a Fire-type on a furnace gets the Stoker, and a Psychic-type on a chest and a Water-type on a cauldron get no role. A role file that turns on the auto-assign flag of Telekinesis still gives no order, because Telekinesis names two targets. Fails if: a result differs. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-04 | If the Cobblemon has no Fairy type and the aimed block fits only roles that CD-QWK-03 leaves out, then the work engine MUST NOT start an order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{target} needs the Work Leash". | MUST NOT | R63, R29 | The approver: Quick Work "shouldn't work with machines that require the cobblemon dynamics leash item". The message tells the player why the send-out stayed a normal one. | GameTest: a Psychic-type sent out at a chest, and a Water-type sent out at a cauldron. Fails if: either gets an order, or a message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-05 | The work engine MUST NOT give a Quick Work order to a Cobblemon with a Fairy type for a role that does not opt in to Quick Work (CD-QWK-36), and a Quick Worker takes no heal claim. | MUST NOT | R63, R13, R18, R70 | A Fairy-type with no binding is a healer, and no auto claim gives it a machine (CD-ASG-04). A healer heals only in its own pasture (ruling 2). By R70, a Fairy-type works an enchanting table as the Muse on a Quick Work order, because the Muse's role file opts in. | Unit: the pick rule gives a Water/Fairy Cobblemon no role at a water wheel, and a Fairy-type the Muse at an enchanting table. GameTest: a Water/Fairy Azumarill sent out at a dry water wheel stays out for 200 ticks. Fails if: it gets an order, a claim of any kind or an action-bar message, or the Fairy-type at the enchanting table gets no role. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-06 | If the owner is a spectator, or `ServerPlayer.mayInteract` refuses the owner the target's canonical block, then the work engine MUST NOT start an order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "You may not use {target}". | MUST NOT | R63, ADR 0013, misuse sweep | A ray-traced target makes no click for a protection mod to cancel (CD-SEC-11). `mayInteract` asks the hook that vanilla asks for a player's projectiles, spawn protection and the world border, and it has no side effect (ruling 3). | GameTest: a fake owner whose `mayInteract` refuses the furnace, and an owner in spectator mode, each send out a Charmander at a furnace with raw iron. Fails if: either gets an order, or a message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-07 | If an exclusive claim or an exclusive leash binding already holds the target, then the work engine MUST NOT start an order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{target} is already being worked". The send-out stays a normal one. | MUST NOT | R63, R2, ADR 0005, R66 | One Cobblemon acts on a block at a time (G4), and the order takes a normal work claim (ruling 3). The shared claims and bindings of logistics roles never block it (CD-CLM-11). | GameTest: a pastured Charmander works a furnace, and a player sends out a second Charmander at it. Fails if: the second one gets an order or a claim, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-08 | If the Cobblemon is Tired at the end of its send-out, then the work engine MUST NOT start an order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} is too Tired to work". | MUST NOT | R63, R10 | A Tired Cobblemon works no machine until it is back at 100% (CD-STA-09, ruling 3). | GameTest: save a Charmander's Work Stamina as 0% and Tired, then send it out at a furnace with raw iron. Fails if: it gets an order, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-09 | If the target has no demand when the order would start and the role does not opt in to Quick Work (CD-QWK-36), then the work engine MUST NOT start an order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{target} needs no work now". | MUST NOT | R63, R70 | The block must have demand when the order starts (ruling 2), so a Cobblemon never waits at a machine that needs nothing. | GameTest: send out a Charmander at an empty furnace. Fails if: it gets an order, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-10 | If no work spot of the target passes the spot and path checks of CD-ASG-09, then the work engine MUST NOT start an order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} cannot reach {target}". | MUST NOT | R63, R57 | The order walks to a spot through the existing spot search and its path check (ruling 4). R57 already words the message. | GameTest: a player on a stone roof sends out a Charmander through a hole in the roof at a furnace with raw iron below it, whose four sides are stone. Fails if: it gets an order, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-11 | When an order starts, the work engine MUST send the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} goes to work on {target}". | MUST | R63 | The owner learns at once that the send-out became an order (ruling 4). | GameTest: the scene of CD-QWK-02. Fails if: the owner gets no such message. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-12 | While a Quick Worker holds its order, Cobblemon's follow-owner task MUST NOT move it, because the work activity replaces Cobblemon's idle activity (CD-AI-02). | MUST NOT | R63, ADR 0013 | A party Cobblemon walks to an owner who is more than 5 blocks away, and teleports to one who is more than 32 blocks away, through a task of Cobblemon's idle activity. | GameTest: the owner of CD-QWK-02 stands 10 blocks from furnace A. Fails if: the Charmander leaves its work spot during 200 ticks of Working. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-13 | If the owner's eye position is farther than `quickWork.leashRange` blocks from the box of the aimed block, then the work engine MUST end the order within 1 tick, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} stopped working: you are too far from {target}". | MUST | R63, ADR 0013, limits sweep | The approver: "with it leashing away from the smelter if I walk too far away". The trace and the leash measure from the same eye position, so a new order never starts beyond its leash. | GameTest: the owner of CD-QWK-02 moves to 18 blocks from furnace A while the Charmander works. Fails if: the order or its claim still exists 2 ticks later, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-14 | After an order ends, the work engine MUST NOT start another order or claim for that Cobblemon until its owner sends it out again with a new aim, and it keeps no slot, claim, order or aim of the ended order. | MUST NOT | R63, ADR 0013 | The approver: "it shouldn't keep assessing for work after being leashed away and/or recalled; so it shouldn't take TPS for that" (ruling 4). | GameTest: after the end of CD-QWK-13, the owner comes back to 6 blocks from furnace A and looks at it for 200 ticks. Fails if: the Charmander claims the furnace again, or the engine holds a slot, a claim, an order or an aim for it. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-15 | When an order ends, the work engine MUST clear the Cobblemon's work order, so that the Cobblemon returns to Cobblemon's follow-owner behavior. | MUST | R63, ADR 0013 | After the end, the Cobblemon acts as any sent-out party Cobblemon does (ruling 4). | GameTest: after the end of CD-QWK-13, wait 100 ticks. Fails if: the Charmander's Brain still holds a work order, or the Charmander has not come at least 2 blocks closer to its owner. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-16 | If the Quick Worker's entity is removed, dead or in Cobblemon's recall animation, or is no longer the Cobblemon's sent-out entity, then the work engine MUST end the order within 1 tick, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} stopped working: it was recalled". | MUST | R63, ADR 0013 | A recall ends the work (ruling 4). Cobblemon's recall animation lasts 1.5 s, and the worker stops at its start. | GameTest: the owner of CD-QWK-02 sends the send-out packet again, which recalls the working Charmander. Fails if: the order or its claim still exists 2 ticks later, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-17 | When a Quick Worker becomes Tired, the work engine MUST end the order in the same tick, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} is Tired and stopped working". | MUST | R63, R10 | A Tired Cobblemon stops (ruling 4). It takes no bed, because no pasture holds it, and it recovers at the base rate (CD-STA-11). | GameTest: save a level 10 Charmander's Work Stamina as 0.1%, then send it out at a furnace with raw iron. Fails if: the order still exists 1 tick after the Charmander is Tired, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-18 | When the work claim of a Quick Worker ends because its target breaks, is replaced, no longer fits its role, unloads or goes to a leash binding, the work engine MUST end the order within 1 tick, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} stopped working: {target} can no longer be worked". | MUST | R63, ADR 0005 | The order works one block, once (ruling 4). The usual claim checks release the claim (CD-CLM-05, CD-CLM-08). | GameTest: break furnace A while the Charmander works. Fails if: the order still exists 2 ticks later, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-19 | If a Quick Worker whose role does not opt in to Quick Work (CD-QWK-36) has been in Standby for `engine.standbyMoveTicks` (default 200), then the work engine MUST end the order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} stopped working: {target} needs no more work". | MUST | R63, R44, R70 | The usual Standby time ends the order, where a pasture worker would move to another target (CD-ASG-19, ruling 4). | GameTest: a furnace with 1 raw iron. Fails if: the order ends before 200 ticks of Standby after the iron smelts, still runs after 201 ticks of Standby, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-20 | If the owner logs out or is in another level than the order, then the work engine MUST end the order within 1 tick, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} stopped working: you left its dimension". | MUST | R63 | The leash needs the owner in the order's level (ruling 4). | Unit: the end rule gives this reason for an owner who is offline or in another level. GameTest: remove the fake owner from the server's player list while its Charmander works. Fails if: either check fails, or the order still exists 2 ticks later. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-21 | The work engine MUST NOT run a Quick Work order for a Cobblemon in a battle: a send-out while its owner battles keeps no aim, and a battle ends a running order within 1 tick with the action-bar message "{Pokémon} stopped working for a battle". | MUST NOT | R63 | Battles send Cobblemon out through the same Cobblemon code, and a battling Cobblemon belongs to its battle (ruling 4). | Unit: the trace range rule gives no trace for an owner in a battle, and the end rule ends an order when the Cobblemon battles. Demo: the owner of a Quick Worker that Works a furnace starts a battle with a wild Cobblemon. Fails if: either unit check fails, or the order does not end with the message "{Pokémon} stopped working for a battle". | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-22 | If a Quick Worker has not reached its work spot `engine.travelTimeoutTicks` (default 600) after its claim, then the work engine MUST end the order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "{Pokémon} cannot reach {target}". | MUST | R63, R57 | The order never tries again, because it never searches again (ruling 4). The message is the leash's (R57). | GameTest: wall the Charmander in right after its claim. Fails if: the order still exists 601 ticks after the claim, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-23 | Each tick of a running Quick Work order MUST cost no more than the tick of one pasture worker, the end checks of CD-QWK-13 to CD-QWK-21 and, on every 20th tick of the order, the permission checks of CD-QWK-29, with no scan pass and no search for other targets. | MUST | R63, ADR 0013, quality sweep | The approver: "it shouldn't take TPS". Ruling 5 sets the cost to about one pasture worker's cost. | Inspect: the order's tick calls the steps of a pasture worker's tick, reads its owner by UUID and its own claim, asks the claim checks only on every 20th tick of the order and only for its own target, and starts no scan pass. Fails if: it scans, iterates block positions, asks a claim check on another tick, or reads a target other than its own. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-24 | While no Quick Work order runs, Quick Work MUST add no server time per tick beyond one check per level tick that no order exists. | MUST | R63, ADR 0013, quality sweep | Ruling 5: no cost per tick after an order ends. | Inspect: with no order, the level tick runs no other Quick Work step. Analysis: run the reference world of CD-PERF-01, which has no order, three times on the commit before Quick Work and three times on the Quick Work commit. Fails if: the level tick runs more Quick Work than the check, or the highest mean Dynamics time of the Quick Work runs is more than 0.02 ms per tick above the highest mean of the earlier runs. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-25 | The work engine MUST key each Quick Work order and aim by the Cobblemon's UUID with its owner's UUID, aim only for a player's own party Cobblemon, send each message to that owner only, and keep no order or aim past its end, its owner's logout or the server stop. | MUST | R63, ADR 0013, misuse sweep | Approver, 2026-11-10: "this MUST work in multiplayer". Many players send out Cobblemon on one server, and an integrated server starts again in the same JVM (ruling 6). | Inspect: read the order code against the multiplayer rules of the Phase 2 plan. Fails if: a rule does not hold, an order or an aim outlives its end, its owner's logout or the server stop, or a message goes to another player. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-26 | Where a claim mod is loaded, if that claim mod refuses the owner a right-click with an empty main hand on the target's canonical block, then the work engine MUST NOT start an order, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "You may not use {target}". | MUST NOT | R64, ADR 0013, misuse sweep | The approver: "yes, respect claims; must work with parties/clans (share chunks)". A claim mod that only cancels clicks never sees Quick Work, because the server traces the view and makes no click (CD-QWK-06). The engine asks the claim mod after `mayInteract` and before the checks of CD-QWK-07 to CD-QWK-09, so a refused owner learns nothing about the block. | GameTest: the test mod's claim provider refuses furnace A, and the owner of CD-QWK-02 sends out a Charmander at furnace A. Then the provider allows furnace A, and the owner sends out a second Charmander at it. Fails if: the first Charmander gets an order or a claim, the message is missing, or the second Charmander gets no order. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-27 | Where FTB Chunks is loaded, the compat component MUST refuse a Quick Work order exactly when FTB Chunks prevents the owner's right-click with an empty main hand on the target's canonical block. | MUST | R64, RES6 | FTB Chunks decides through the team that owns the claim: its members, and its allies while the team's block interact mode is `allies` and the server's `ally_mode` lets the team choose them. Both are the defaults, and COHO 2.4.0 runs them (RES6). The compat asks the check that FTB Chunks runs for a right-click, so the engine copies no team rule (R64 ruling 2). | GameTest: in the FTB Chunks run, the party of player A claims the chunk of furnace A. B is a member of the party, C is an ally of it, and D is a stranger. In separate tests, A, B, C and D each send out a Charmander at furnace A, and C sends one out again after the party sets its block interact mode to `private`. Fails if: A, B or C gets no order in the default mode, D gets an order or no "You may not use Furnace", or C gets an order in the `private` mode. | compat | agreed |
| CD-QWK-28 | Where Open Parties and Claims is loaded, the compat component MUST refuse a Quick Work order exactly when Open Parties and Claims protects the target's canonical block from the owner's right-click with an empty main hand. | MUST | R64, RES6 | Open Parties and Claims decides through the player config of the claim's owner. By default, its exception `fullAccess` gives full access to the owner's party, and a party that the owner's party allies gets access only when the owner sets that exception to allies. The compat asks the mod's own check of a block interaction, with its messages off, so the engine copies no party rule (R64 ruling 2). | GameTest: in the Open Parties and Claims run, player A claims the chunk of furnace A. B is a member of A's party, C is a member of a party that A's party allies, and D is a stranger. In separate tests, A, B, C and D each send out a Charmander at furnace A, and C sends one out again after A sets `claims.protection.exceptions.fullAccess` to `A`. Fails if: A or B gets no order, C gets an order before the change or no order after it, or D gets an order or no "You may not use Furnace". | compat | agreed |
| CD-QWK-29 | If the owner loses the permission of CD-QWK-06, CD-QWK-26, CD-QWK-27 or CD-QWK-28 while an order runs, then the work engine MUST end the order within 20 ticks, and it sends the owner the action-bar message "You may not use {target}". | MUST | R64, state × event sweep, misuse sweep | R64 ruling 3: a member who leaves the party, or a chunk that another team claims, stops the work. The engine asks the checks of the start again on every 20th tick of the order, so each order costs one claim lookup every 20 ticks. | GameTest: the test mod's claim provider starts to refuse furnace A while a Charmander works it. In the FTB Chunks run, member B leaves the party while B's Charmander works furnace A, and in a second test a stranger's party claims the chunk of a running order. In the Open Parties and Claims run, A's party removes member B while B's Charmander works furnace A. Fails if: an order still exists 21 ticks after the change, or the message is missing. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-30 | If a claim mod's check throws an `Exception` or a `LinkageError`, then the work engine MUST count the check as a refusal, and it logs one warning per cause. | MUST | R64, failure sweep, CD-PERF-05 | R64 ruling 4: the check fails closed. An update of a claim mod that renames a method must not let strangers work machines in its claims, and a refused send-out stays a normal one. One warning per cause keeps the log readable. | Unit: a claim gate with fake checks: a `RuntimeException`, a `NoSuchMethodError` and a `NoClassDefFoundError` each give a refusal, one cause twice logs one warning, and a second cause logs a second warning. GameTest: the test mod's claim provider throws for furnace A, and the owner sends out a Charmander at furnace A. Fails if: a check that throws allows an order, the Charmander gets an order or no "You may not use Furnace", or a cause logs twice. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-QWK-31 | The compat component MUST ask each claim mod about the owner's own `ServerPlayer`, which `PlayerList.getPlayer` returns for the owner's UUID, and never about a fake player. | MUST | R64, RES6, misuse sweep | A Quick Work order acts for its owner, so the owner's own rights decide. COHO's FTB Chunks sets `fake_players` to `check`, which applies each team's fake player settings to fake players such as block breakers, and Architectury counts every subclass of `ServerPlayer` as a fake player (RES6). | Inspect: each compat check passes on the `ServerPlayer` that the engine gives it, and the engine gives the one that `PlayerList.getPlayer` returns for the owner's UUID. GameTest: the owners of the CD-QWK-27 and CD-QWK-28 tests are of the exact class `ServerPlayer`. Fails if: a check builds or passes another player, or a test owner is a subclass of `ServerPlayer`. | compat | agreed |
| CD-QWK-32 | When a claim mod refuses an order, the work engine MUST send its action-bar message after any message of the claim mod in that tick, so that the owner's action bar shows "You may not use {target}". | MUST | R64, RES6 | The check of FTB Chunks shows "Interaction prevented here by claim protection!" on the action bar, at most once every 2 s for each player, and it has no way to stay silent. A newer action-bar message replaces the older one. The check of Open Parties and Claims sends nothing while its `messages` argument is false. | GameTest: stranger D of CD-QWK-27 and stranger D of CD-QWK-28 record the packets that they get. Fails if: the last action-bar packet that D gets in the refusal tick is not "You may not use Furnace", or the Open Parties and Claims run sends D a chat message. | compat | agreed |
| CD-QWK-33 | Where a claim mod is not loaded, the mod MUST load no class of that claim mod's compat package. | MUST | R64, failure sweep | A class that names a missing mod crashes the game (CD-CMP-06). As for Create, only a `register` call behind `ModList.isLoaded` names each compat package (R64 ruling 1). | GameTest: the test pack's `compat_ftbchunks` server, with FTB Chunks and without Open Parties and Claims, loads `compat.ftbchunks.FtbChunksCompat` and no class of `compat.opac`. The `compat_opac` server does the reverse, and the `compat_cobblebase` and `compat_cobbleworkers` servers load neither package. Fails if: a class of an absent mod's compat package loads, or an error names one. | compat | agreed |
| CD-QWK-34 | The mod MUST declare `ftbchunks` and `openpartiesandclaims` as optional dependencies with the version range `*`. | MUST | R64 | Both claim mods stay optional (R64 ruling 1), and the coordinator's ruling on the design lists them as Cobbleworkers and Cobblebase are listed. NeoForge then checks no version of them, and a check that fails refuses (CD-QWK-30). A pack without them runs Quick Work with the checks of CD-QWK-06 only. | Unit: `neoforge.mods.toml` has an entry for `ftbchunks` and one for `openpartiesandclaims`, each with `type = "optional"`, `versionRange = "*"` and `side = "BOTH"`, and no entry for `ftbteams`. Fails if: an entry is missing, is required or has another range, or FTB Teams has an entry. | build | agreed |
| CD-QWK-35 | The server config MUST NOT hold a key that turns off the claim checks of CD-QWK-26 to CD-QWK-29. | MUST NOT | R64 | R64 ruling 5: where a claim mod is loaded, Quick Work respects its claims. A server that wants no Quick Work keeps `quickWork.enabled` (CD-CFG-16). | Unit: the keys of the server config equal the list of CD-CFG-01, which holds no key for claims. Fails if: the config has a key that the list lacks. | build | agreed |
| CD-QWK-36 | Where a role file sets `quick_work` to true, the work engine MUST give that role Quick Work orders although its auto-assign flag is false and no pasture scan visits its handler: a Fairy-type gets an order for it (CD-QWK-05), the order starts while the target has no demand (CD-QWK-09), and its worker waits in Standby with no Standby end (CD-QWK-19), while the other ends of the order hold. | MUST | R70 | The approver: "fairy types should be able to be thrown out from inventory at enchantment tables to grant it the bonus that they'd normally grant in the pasture/working (similarly to how fire tpyes can be thrown out at furnances to power them from player inventory)". The Muse's role file sets the flag, and no other default role file does. The owner cannot press R with a screen open, so the table's menu is closed at the send-out, and a player often closes the table to fetch lapis or books and comes back. A role that names two targets keeps the rule of CD-QWK-03. | Unit: `QuickWorkRulesTest` gives a Fairy-type the Muse at an enchanting table and no role at a water wheel, gives no order for a role with the flag that names two targets, starts an order of the Muse with no demand, and ends it for every reason but Standby. `RoleCodecTest` reads the flag with the default false, and `BuiltInRoleFilesTest` finds it only in `muse.json`. GameTest: a Lv50 Clefairy sent out at a closed enchanting table gets an order and waits in Standby. A player opens the table's menu with a pickaxe in it, and the Clefairy Works within 20 ticks, with the offers of a table with 8 bookshelves. The menu closes, and the order still holds 210 ticks later. Fails if: a result differs, the Clefairy gets no order, it does not Work while the menu is open, the offers lack the bonus, or the order ends while the Clefairy waits. | work engine | agreed |

### LND: land claims of pastures and MineColonies (Phase 3)

| ID | Requirement | Level | Source | Rationale | Acceptance test | Owner | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-LND-01 | Where a claim mod is loaded, the work engine MUST NOT offer an auto-assigned worker of a Work Mode pasture a target whose canonical block that claim mod refuses in the pasture check. | MUST NOT | R67, Q9, ADR 0014, misuse sweep | The feature that the approver approved (R67): "The auto-assignment and the workers of a pasture must never work a target in a chunk that the pasture's owner may not interact with." The work area of a pasture can reach into the land claim of another player. The engine asks once for each target of a scan pass, before it offers the targets to the workers. | GameTest: the test mod's claim provider refuses furnace B to the placer of a Work Mode pasture. The work area holds furnaces A and B, each with 64 raw iron, and two Charmanders join the pasture. After 400 ticks, the provider allows furnace B. Fails if: a worker claims furnace B in the first 400 ticks, furnace A gets no worker, or no worker claims furnace B within 400 ticks after the provider allows it. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-LND-02 | If the pasture check of a loaded claim mod starts to refuse a target while a worker of the pasture holds a work claim or a bed claim on it, then the work engine MUST release that claim within 20 ticks. | MUST | R67, ADR 0014, state × event sweep | A member who leaves a party, a chunk that a stranger claims, or a rank that a colony lowers ends the right of the placer. A pasture polls its workers every 20 ticks (CD-ASG-15), and each poll asks the pasture check again for each claim of its workers. The released worker goes back to Idle, and CD-LND-01 keeps it off the target. | GameTest: a Charmander of a Work Mode pasture Works furnace A, and the test mod's claim provider starts to refuse furnace A to the placer. In a second test, a Tired Charmander sleeps on bed A, and the provider starts to refuse bed A. Fails if: a claim on furnace A or bed A remains 21 ticks after the refusal, or a worker claims furnace A again while the provider refuses it. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-LND-03 | If the pasture check of a loaded claim mod refuses the bound target or the Telekinesis destination of a worker's leash binding, then the work engine MUST clear the binding within 20 ticks through the leash resets. | MUST | R67, R58, ADR 0014, state × event sweep | A binding reserves its target across all pastures (ADR 0005). A binding on a block that the placer may not use would keep the workers of the land claim's own players off that block. The binder's click passed the claim mod when the binder made the binding (CD-SEC-11), but the right can end later. The binder gets the chat message of CD-LSH-13 with the reason "the owner of its pasture may not use its target" or "the owner of its pasture may not use its destination", also at the next login. | GameTest: a Charmander bound to furnace A Works it, and the test mod's claim provider starts to refuse furnace A to the placer. In a second test, the provider starts to refuse the destination chest of a bound Telekinesis worker. Fails if: a binding remains 21 ticks after the refusal, the binder gets no chat message with the reason of the row, or the worker still holds a claim. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-LND-04 | The work engine MUST NOT give a Tired worker of a Work Mode pasture a bed claim on a bed that the pasture check of a loaded claim mod refuses. | MUST NOT | R67, ADR 0014, misuse sweep | A bed is a target (Terms), and a bed claim holds the bed against the workers of the land claim's own players. A Tired worker with no allowed bed in its work area rests in place, as with no unclaimed bed (CD-BED-08). | GameTest: the test mod's claim provider refuses bed A to the placer, and a Tired Charmander of the pasture has bed A at 3 blocks and bed B at 6 blocks. In a second test, bed A is the only bed. Fails if: the Charmander claims bed A in either test, it does not claim bed B in the first test, or it is not in RestingInPlace in the second test. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-LND-05 | The pasture check MUST ask each claim mod about the UUID of the pasture's placer, the `ownerId` of the pasture block entity, whether the placer is online or not, and never about a `ServerPlayer`. | MUST | R67, ADR 0014, actor × action sweep | R67: "The owner may be offline, so the check must work from the owner's UUID". The placer is the one source of pasture ownership (Terms), and the placer turns Work Mode on (CD-WMD-03). A pasture with no placer has no UUID, so each claim mod refuses it every block of its land claims. | Inspect: each pasture check takes a UUID and no player, and the engine passes the `ownerId` that the last poll read. GameTest: the test mod's claim provider records the UUIDs that it is asked about, and it refuses furnace B to the placer, a test owner whom `PlayerList.getPlayer` does not return. Fails if: the provider is asked about another UUID or gets a player, or a worker claims furnace B. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-LND-06 | Where FTB Chunks is loaded, its pasture check MUST refuse a target exactly when FTB Chunks prevents a right-click with an empty main hand by the placer on the target's canonical block, decided from the placer's UUID. | MUST | R67, RES7, ADR 0014 | FTB Chunks 2101.1.23 has no check by UUID. `shouldPreventInteraction` needs a `ServerPlayer`, allows every other actor and shows the player its own text (RES7). The check takes the steps of that method through the APIs of FTB Chunks and FTB Teams: `disable_protection`, the placer's admin bypass, the wilderness, the tag `ftbchunks:interact_whitelist`, and the team's block interact mode. That mode reads `ChunkTeamData.isAlly`, which applies `ally_mode`, or a rank of member or better. On COHO, the members and allies of the claiming team may use its blocks, and a stranger may not. | GameTest: in the FTB Chunks run, the party of player A claims the chunk of furnace A. B is a member of the party, C is an ally of it, and D is a stranger, each an online player of the exact class `ServerPlayer`. The check runs for each player on furnace A, for C after the party sets its block interact mode to `private`, and for D after the party sets it to `public`. It also runs for D on a crafting table in the claim, for D with the admin bypass on, and for D on a furnace in an unclaimed chunk. Then each player logs out, and every check runs again. Fails if: an answer differs from `!shouldPreventInteraction(player, InteractionHand.MAIN_HAND, pos, Protection.INTERACT_BLOCK, null)` for the online player, or an answer changes after the logout. | compat | agreed |
| CD-LND-07 | Where Open Parties and Claims is loaded, its pasture check MUST refuse a target exactly when the claim of the canonical block's chunk gives the placer's UUID no full access. | MUST | R67, RES7, ADR 0014 | `IChunkProtectionAPI.hasChunkAccess(UUID, ResourceLocation, int, int)` answers by UUID, also for an offline player (RES7). It reads the player config of the claim's owner: `claims.protectClaimedChunks`, the group of `claims.protection.exceptions.fullAccess`, and the access overriders of other mods. By default the group is `P`, the owner's party, and `A` adds the allied parties. A player without full access can still use the blocks of the mod's exception lists, such as the crafting table. The check refuses those blocks, and no role works one of them. | GameTest: in the Open Parties and Claims run, player A claims the chunk of furnace A. B is a member of A's party, C is a member of a party that A's party allies, and D is a stranger, each an online player. The check runs for each player on furnace A, and again for C after A sets `claims.protection.exceptions.fullAccess` to `A`. Then each player logs out, and every check runs again. Fails if: an answer differs from `!onBlockInteraction(player, InteractionHand.MAIN_HAND, ItemStack.EMPTY, level, pos, Direction.UP, false, false)` for the online player, or an answer changes after the logout. | compat | agreed |
| CD-LND-08 | Where MineColonies is loaded, its pasture check MUST refuse a target inside a colony exactly when the colony's own right-click rule refuses the rank that `IPermissions.getRank(UUID)` gives the placer. | MUST | R67, R68, RES7, ADR 0014 | R68: a colony's own ranks decide who may use a block. The check follows `ColonyPermissionEventHandler.onPlayerInteract` of 1.1.1419 for a right-click with an empty main hand (RES7). A hut block needs `ACCESS_HUTS`. A block of `ModTags.colonyProtectionException` passes for a rank that is not hostile. While `enableColonyProtection` is true, any other block needs `RIGHTCLICK_BLOCK`, and a block with an item handler also needs `OPEN_CONTAINER`. A player outside the colony's list has the Neutral rank. The API has no getter for a colony's free blocks, so the check refuses them. The operator bypass needs a player in creative mode at the click, so it never applies to a pasture. | GameTest: in the MineColonies run, player A owns a colony around furnace A and an enchanting table. B is an Officer, C a Friend, D a stranger and E Hostile, each an online player. The check runs for each player on both blocks with the default rank permissions, and again for C after A gives the Friend rank `OPEN_CONTAINER`. Then each player logs out, and every check runs again. Fails if: an answer differs from the cancellation of a `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickBlock` with an empty main hand that the test posts for the online player, or an answer changes after the logout. | compat | agreed |
| CD-LND-09 | Where MineColonies is loaded, its Quick Work check MUST refuse an order exactly when MineColonies cancels a right-click with an empty main hand by the owner on the target's canonical block. | MUST | R67, R68, RES7, ADR 0014 | Quick Work makes no click (CD-QWK-06), so the colony's event handler never sees an order. The check applies the rule of CD-LND-08 through `IPermissions.hasPermission(Player, Action)`. That call also lets an operator pass whose permission level is at least `permissionEventMinBypassPermLevel` (COHO: 2) while in creative mode, as the owner's own click would. The exception of `pvp_mode` for attacking players stays out, and COHO turns `pvp_mode` off. CD-QWK-26, CD-QWK-29 and CD-QWK-30 apply to this check as to every claim mod. | GameTest: in the MineColonies run, the owner of CD-QWK-02 holds each rank of CD-LND-08 in turn and sends out a Charmander at furnace A in the colony. Then the owner is an operator of level 2, once in creative mode and once in survival mode. In a last test, A lowers the owner from Officer to Neutral while the order runs. Fails if: an order starts where the test's posted `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickBlock` is canceled, no order starts where the event is not canceled, a refusal lacks "You may not use Furnace", or the order still runs 21 ticks after the rank change. | compat | agreed |
| CD-LND-10 | The pasture check MUST NOT send a message to a player or count an attempt in a claim mod. | MUST NOT | R67, ADR 0014, misuse sweep | A scan pass asks about many targets. The player check of FTB Chunks shows its text on the player's action bar, and the event handler of MineColonies messages the player and counts the player's attempts. The pasture check calls neither. Only the leash resets message a binder (CD-LND-03). | Inspect: the pasture checks of the three compat packages call no `shouldPreventInteraction` and no `onBlockInteraction`, and they post no event. GameTest: in each claim run, the placer of a Work Mode pasture is an online stranger to the land claim of furnace A, and records its packets for 400 ticks while the scan passes refuse furnace A. Fails if: the placer gets a chat or action-bar packet. | compat | agreed |
| CD-LND-11 | If a pasture check throws an `Exception` or a `LinkageError`, then the work engine MUST count the check as a refusal, and it logs one warning per cause. | MUST | R67, ADR 0014, failure sweep, CD-PERF-05 | An update of a claim mod that renames a method must not let the workers of pastures into its claims. The pastures of that server then work no target until a release of Dynamics follows the change, and the warning names the claim mod and the error. One warning per cause keeps the log readable. | Unit: a claim gate with fake checks: a `RuntimeException`, a `NoSuchMethodError` and a `NoClassDefFoundError` from a pasture check each give a refusal, one cause twice logs one warning, and the warning of a pasture check differs from that of a Quick Work check. GameTest: the test mod's claim provider throws for furnace A, and furnace A is in the work area of a Charmander's pasture. Fails if: a check that throws allows a claim, the Charmander claims furnace A, or a cause logs twice. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-LND-12 | The pasture checks MUST cost at most one check of each loaded claim mod for each target that a scan pass offers, and one for each claim, bound target and Telekinesis destination of a worker on each poll of its pasture, and one for each block that a harvest changes, in the tick of the harvest (CD-HRV-13). | MUST | R67, ADR 0014, quality sweep, CD-PERF-01 | A pasture polls every 20 ticks (CD-ASG-15) and scans every `scanIntervalTicks`. With 64 Working workers, the polls ask about 3.2 blocks per tick. Each check reads the maps of a claim mod: a chunk claim and a team rank, a claim config and a party, or a chunk's colony and a rank. | Inspect: the engine asks the pasture check only in the assignment after a scan pass, in the poll, in the choice of a bed and in the harvest check of CD-HRV-13. Analysis: the perf run of CD-PERF-01, whose test mod registers the test mod's claim provider, measures the mean and the p95 of the server time of Dynamics per tick. Fails if: a check runs at another point, runs for a block that is not a target, or the run exceeds 1.0 ms mean or 2.0 ms p95. | work engine | agreed |
| CD-LND-13 | Where MineColonies is not loaded, the mod MUST load no class of the compat package `compat.minecolonies`. | MUST | R67, ADR 0014, failure sweep | A class that names a missing mod crashes the game (CD-CMP-06). As for the other claim mods (CD-QWK-33), only a `register` call behind `ModList.isLoaded` names the package. | Unit: only files under `compat/minecolonies/` name `com.minecolonies` or `com.ldtteam`, and the mod class calls `MineColoniesCompat.register()` only behind `ModList.get().isLoaded("minecolonies")`. GameTest: the test pack's `compat_minecolonies` server, with the MineColonies jars of R68, loads `compat.minecolonies.MineColoniesCompat` and no class of `compat.ftbchunks` or `compat.opac`. The `compat_ftbchunks`, `compat_opac`, `compat_cobblebase` and `compat_cobbleworkers` servers load no class of `compat.minecolonies`. Fails if: a class of an absent mod's compat package loads, or an error names one. | compat | agreed |

### CUL: Cultivator (1.1)

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-CUL-01 | The role registry MUST enable the Cultivator role, with the handler `cobblemon_dynamics:cultivator`, for the Ground type, with its auto-assign flag on. | MUST | R72, G12, ADR 0015 | The approver: "I was hoping to make the other one have the function of Bonemeal/quick growth for applicable crops in range". Rock keeps the Crystal Tender (ruling 1 of R72), so the Ground type grows crops (ruling 2), and the auto-assign flag lets a Cultivator "go crop to crop" with no leash. | Unit: `BuiltInRoleFilesTest` reads `cultivator.json`: handler `cobblemon_dynamics:cultivator`, types `ground`, `auto_assign` true, priority 0, first animation `mudsport`. GameTest: in the scene of CD-CRP-10, a Diglett claims a wheat crop with the role `cobblemon_dynamics:cultivator`. Fails if: a value differs, or the Diglett takes another role or no claim. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-CUL-02 | The Cultivator handler MUST match a block only when the block is in `#cobblemon_dynamics:cultivator_targets` and a crop adapter grows it (CD-CRP-03). | MUST | R73, ADR 0015 | Ruling 5 of R73: the tags decide which blocks a role works, so that a pack adds its own crops. A tagged block that no adapter reads gets no worker (CD-CRP-04). | Unit: `CropAdapterChoiceTest`: the facts of wheat (a `CropBlock`) and of a sweet berry bush (a `BonemealableBlock`) get a growth adapter, and the facts of nether wart (no `BonemealableBlock`) get none. Fails if: a block with no growth adapter matches, or a growable crop of the tag does not. | target handler (farming) | agreed |
| CD-CUL-03 | The Cultivator handler MUST report demand only while its crop's block is a valid bonemeal target and the crop's harvest adapter, where one exists, does not find the crop fully grown. | MUST | R72, RES8, ADR 0015 | The approver: "using bonemeal until the crop is fully grown and move on". `BonemealableBlock.isValidBonemealTarget` is false for a fully grown `CropBlock` (RES8). The harvest adapter stops the Cultivator where the Harvester starts, on a crop that bonemeal could still change, such as a ripe tomato under a rope. | Unit: `CropRulesTest`: a valid target that is not fully grown has demand, a valid target that its harvest adapter finds fully grown has none, and an invalid target has none. GameTest: in the scene of CD-CRP-10, the Diglett leaves each wheat crop once it reaches age 7. Fails if: a fully grown crop has demand, or a growing crop has none. | target handler (farming) | agreed |
| CD-CUL-04 | While a Cultivator is Working, the handler MUST apply one growth use to its crop every ceil(1,200 ÷ ceil(U × L ÷ 100)) ticks, counted from the tick that its work started, where L is the worker's level and U is the role file's `uses_per_minute_at_level_100` (default 30). | MUST | R72, R75, ADR 0015, limits sweep | Ruling 2 of R72: "at a pace that scales with level". The form follows the item rates of Telekinesis and the Magnet (ceil(64 × L ÷ 100)). Level 1 gives 1 use per minute, level 50 gives 15 and level 100 gives 30. | Unit: `GrowthPaceTest`: the interval is 1,200 ticks at level 1, 80 at level 50 and 40 at level 100. At level 100, uses fall on ticks 0, 40 and 80 after the start and not on tick 39. Fails if: an interval differs, or a use falls between two use ticks. | target handler (farming) | agreed |
| CD-CUL-05 | When a Cultivator applies a growth use, the handler MUST call NeoForge's `BoneMealItem.applyBonemeal` on the crop with a new bone meal stack that no inventory holds and a null player. | MUST | R72, RES8, ADR 0015 | The approver: "does not use bonemeal item, just stamina and produces the bonemeal effect". The call runs the crop's own bonemeal rule and NeoForge's `BonemealEvent`, as a dispenser does through `BoneMealItem.growCrop` (RES8), so a mod that changes or blocks bonemeal on its crops still decides. The stack is a token: no player, container or worker loses an item. | Inspect: the handler's only growth call is `BoneMealItem.applyBonemeal(ItemStack(Items.BONE_MEAL), level, pos, null)`. GameTest: in the scene of CD-CRP-10, a listener of the test mod counts the `BonemealEvent`s at the wheat. Fails if: a use posts no event, an event names a player, or an inventory of the scene loses an item. | target handler (farming) | agreed |
| CD-CUL-06 | When the Cultivator applies a growth use to a valid bonemeal target, the stamina component MUST drain `stamina_ticks_per_use` ticks of work capacity (default 100), beyond the drain of CD-STA-06. | MUST | R72, R75, ADR 0008, ADR 0015 | Ruling 2 of R72: "costing Work Stamina per use". At level 100, 30 uses per minute drain 3,000 ticks beyond the 1,200 ticks of Working, so a Cultivator tires about 3.5 times as fast as a Stoker of its level. Rest stays the cost of every worker (G6). | Unit: `GrowthCostTest`: a level 10 worker at 100% that Works for 40 ticks and applies 1 use reads 100% − 140 ÷ 36,000. GameTest: in the scene of CD-CRP-10, the Diglett's Work Stamina falls from the tick before its first use to the tick of its second use by the Working ticks between them plus 200 ticks of capacity, ± 1 tick. A crop at age 0 always takes a second use, because one use adds at most 5 ages. Fails if: a use drains nothing, or drains another amount. | stamina | agreed |
| CD-CUL-07 | When a growth use returns true, the handler MUST send 15 `minecraft:happy_villager` particles inside the crop's block through `ServerLevel.sendParticles`, and post no level event. | MUST | R72, RES8, CD-FX-09 | The approver asked for "the bonemeal effect". The particle packet shows the same green sparkles with no sound. | Inspect: the handler calls `sendParticles` with `ParticleTypes.HAPPY_VILLAGER` and a count of 15, and `SilenceTest` finds no `levelEvent` in the farming sources (CD-CRP-14). Demo: check 61. Fails if: the particle or the count differs, a level event is posted, or the Demo shows no sparkles at a use. | effects | agreed |
| CD-CUL-08 | The Cultivator handler MUST read `uses_per_minute_at_level_100` (default 30) and `stamina_ticks_per_use` (default 100) from its role file. | MUST | R72, R75, ADR 0006, ADR 0010 | Role numbers live in role files, so a pack tunes the pace and the cost with a datapack (CD-CFG-07), and the BAL rows check the defaults. | Unit: `BuiltInRoleFilesTest` reads 30 and 100 from `cultivator.json`, and `GrowthPaceTest` gives an interval of 80 ticks at level 100 for parameters with 15. Fails if: a default differs, or the pace or the cost ignores the file. | role registry | agreed |
| CD-CUL-09 | If 20 growth uses in a row leave every block of a crop unchanged, then the Cultivator handler MUST report no demand on that crop to that worker for 6,000 ticks. | MUST | R72, RES8, failure sweep | Some blocks stay valid bonemeal targets where bonemeal can do nothing more: rice under a blocked cell, the top of a tomato vine, or a spreading plant that a pack adds (RES8). Fails if: the stop comes earlier or later, or never ends. | target handler (farming) | agreed |
| CD-CUL-10 | The default tag `cobblemon_dynamics:cultivator_targets` MUST NOT hold Cobblemon's apricorns or terrain seeds. | MUST NOT | R72, R75, RES8, ADR 0015 | The approver excluded apricorns from the harvest, and the coordinator's brief says that apricorns are "only to be excluded", so the Cultivator leaves them too. The approver answered Q10 with "no" (R75). | Unit: `FarmingTagFilesTest` resolves the default tag against the tag files of the repository and of the Cobblemon jar, and finds no member of `#cobblemon:apricorns` or `#cobblemon:terrain_seeds`.
